Do not open the gate at the end of nine miles of road, send anybody to it, or re-spend the arithmetic by which it was identified. Do not answer the question from a road, and do not have the keeper of records answer it. Do not resolve the shut road four hundred miles off, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine, and do not answer the offer with a figure. Do not repair the breach between the two men, and do not report the span as a repair. Do not reopen the city, the valley, the press's opening day, the roadmark, the mechanism, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. Do not merge the three bars. Do not take Hushwater and do not print its two figures. Do not add a tenth asking of the bench, a tenth not-asking, an eighth register entry, or a tenth thing to any of the three nines, and do not add any of the nines to a figure of persons. Do not print the corridor's rate as a figure and do not print a rate for the tide road. Do not make the driftwood's notches into tides. Do not fill the fourth head of the form, and do not have a character say that anybody has filled it in somewhere else. Do not say whether the man of about thirty-four with a sheet and the four men with a crowbar are connected. Do not have the nine of Volume 09 meet, be quoted, be sent for, or be put in a room, and do not have a council of nine on the foreshore. Do not have the protagonist hold the middle, conduct anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, or be the reason a thing works. Do not have anybody thank the keeper of records, the woman of about fifty with a board, or the woman of about fifty-eight with a slate, and do not restate either woman's want. Do not compare the nine-mile road with the tide road, and do not send anybody from one to the other. Do not play the accent beat a fifth time in the shape it has already played four times. Do not schedule anything for the first of a month, the tide road, the press, the bell, the seed vault, or Chapter 900. And do not edit `outline/volume-09.md`, whose three wrong rows are owed by whoever owns the plan.**

1150. **And the standing lesson this block hands on, which is not a number.** **A figure that is right for a different year is invisible to every instrument in this project, and the cheapest defect in this block was a boy who was about nine printed as about eleven because the month had moved between the chapter he came from and the chapter he is in.** The second cheapest was a man of about twenty-seven placed at a meeting held before he was born. **Both are the same class as the month that is not a month and the day that is in the wrong month, and none of the three is a count.** A phase that inherits a certificate re-counts the figure the repair is about instead of taking the direction of the certificate's sentence. A state file that reports a sweep is a second measurement of the prose and the second measurement is the one that goes stale. **And a block that edits by script edits by script and then reads the file, and this block's own repairs were applied by script and every one of them was read afterwards, and the six quotation-mark defects that script could not see were found by the one instrument in this project's set that has found a real defect in delivered prose three times and which checks across a block boundary and not inside one.**

## Volume 10, Batch 0002, craft repair pass

**A review of this block read for craft, after the block had already run all nine of its own checks and reported twenty-two repairs and a clean bill, found four things that no instrument in this project can see. This pass fixes them. The highest number in this file is now 1156.**

1151. **The protagonist had no name on the page in twenty chapters, and the prompt had required it.** **`Aren` and `Kest` are both at zero across Chapters 451 to 470 as the block was written, and `workspace/volume-10/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` §6 says in terms: *He is named in this volume and the house form a man of thirty-two with a limp is still the house form.* That is a prompt violation and not a house style, and the reviewer's charge that the male lead had been progressively erased is correct as to this volume and correct as to the five volumes before it. The house form is not itself the defect; the house form used as the *only* form is. `chapter-0469.md` is his one chapter in this block and he is now named in the narration, in a speech attribution, and in the closing frame, and the closing frame was rewritten because it asserted that he never said his name, which the new narration contradicted.** The name budget is untouched by this: `Aren Kest` is not a new name and a return costs nothing, so the arithmetic stands at four of five spent, one slot remaining, `Tobin Kernick` the only name this block spent.

1152. **Chapter 0461 had zero quoted speech in 1,797 words, and it is a chapter about nine people in a room for three hours.** **The chapter reported a conversation instead of printing one, and the reported version is now replaced with the conversation itself: the man of sixty puts the wagon question as four questions and gets three silences, the man of about twenty-seven admits he was not at the barn he has told people he was at, and the boy of nineteen says that writing the slipway's book and not being able to read it is the whole of what he knows and it is enough. The chapter now carries twenty-one quoted spans and 1,963 words.** No event changed. The four clauses, the fourpence, the key on the table at the eighth hour, the boy of about nine, the eleven paces of stone and the twenty-three pence are all as they were.

1153. **The `about` tic, cut from 31.04 per 1,000 to about 16.2, on the basis of a whole-word numerator over a whitespace denominator.** **A previous version of the Batch 0003 prompt said there is no target for a rate of `about` and let five volumes run; the reviewer's point that the project was measuring this instead of fixing it is correct, and the correction is not a numeric target but a reading rule: the hedge stays on a figure the world is honestly vague about — about nine hundred people, about nine hundred paces, about four hours out of twelve, about nine years, about twenty-two years, about nineteen years — and comes off a figure somebody standing in the room could have counted: nine people, four people, nine feet, four hundred yards, forty minutes, about a hundred and four.** Two hundred and three occurrences were removed. **The hedge was wrongly removed from five canon figures by the sweep itself, and a person reading the diffs found them: `about four hours out of twelve` in Chapters 462 and 468, `about forty days of walking` and `about nine years ago` in Chapter 465, `about forty yards` in Chapter 468, and `about nine years` in Chapters 466, 467 and 470. All eight are restored.** This is the block's own item 1141 lesson running the other way: a script that edits prose is not a repair pass until a person has read the result.

1154. **Eight speech-length figures were re-footed, and every one of them was off by exactly the number of words this pass removed from the speech.** Chapter 463's sixty-two and a hundred and sixty-two became sixty-one and a hundred and sixty; Chapter 466's a hundred and five and seventy-six became a hundred and three and seventy-four; Chapter 468's a hundred and five became a hundred and three, and its a hundred and seventy-four was correct already and was checked rather than moved; Chapter 469's a hundred and nine and a hundred and ninety-one became a hundred and eight and a hundred and ninety. **All eleven figures now foot, counted from the printed speech with the attribution excluded and a turn counted as a turn and not as a quoted span, and the same basis is reported in all five state files.** The five were not wrong when they were written and no reader would have caught them, which is the class the prompt's own §5 item 11 has been warning about for three blocks.

1155. **The zero-punctuation run was an accident and the prompt had promoted it to a rule.** **The first four hundred and seventy chapters carry no question mark, no exclamation mark, no semicolon and no em dash, and a previous version of `workspace/volume-10/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` §3 item 3 called that the house convention and told a writer to set a question asked aloud with a full stop. That is now corrected in the prompt, which says in terms that the run was an accident across five volumes and about six hundred and twenty thousand words, that a question asked aloud now takes a question mark, and that there is still no minimum.** Chapter 0461's new dialogue is set with question marks. **This changes no fact about the world, no money column, no date and no canon figure, and it is the one repair in this pass that a successor is most likely to undo by accident, so it is carried in the open-threads list.**

1156. **What this pass did not do, and the four things still owed.** **It did not touch Volume 09 or any earlier volume, so the `about` rate of 26.5 per 1,000 in Volume 09 and 21.2 in Volume 08, the zero-punctuation run in about six hundred and twenty thousand words before Chapter 461, and the twenty chapters of Volume 10 before this pass that had no dialogue of consequence in them, are all still there. Those are four volumes of a rewrite and not a repair pass, and the plan of record is the author's.** It did not change the plot, the calendar, the money, the name budget, the two wants, the sixth answer, the four clauses, the wall, the fourth head, or the antagonist's absence, all of which are exactly as `chapter-0468.md` and `chapter-0469.md` left them. It did not touch `chapter-0460.md`, which belongs to Batch 0001 and still carries one instance of the retired reporting frame, and a batch may not repair a prior batch's prose on a hunch. And `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and was not read for repair and not written.

1157. **The frame, derived twice from the single anchor of day 930, and the two prompt figures that did not reproduce.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month, is the only anchor, and the chain was re-run independently of the prompt and of every printed list and reproduces every anchor the plan prints: 930 is Mudmonth 25 and a Sunday, 1141 is Wolfmonth 26 and a Monday, 1146 is Frostmonth 1 and a Saturday, 1236 is Thawmonth 1 YR 319 and a Friday, 1237 is Thawmonth 2 YR 319 and a Saturday. **Block 0003 is days 1237 to 1284, forty-eight inclusive and forty-seven elapsed, and the ten chapter days are 1237, 1242, 1247, 1253, 1258, 1263, 1270, 1273, 1279 and 1284**, that is the second, seventh, twelfth, eighteenth, twenty-third and twenty-eighth of Thawmonth YR 319 and the fifth, eighth, fourteenth and nineteenth of Mudmonth YR 319, and they are a Saturday, a Thursday, a Tuesday, a Monday, a Saturday, a Thursday, a Thursday, a Sunday, a Saturday and a Thursday. **The weekday half and the day-of-month half were derived separately from the day number and not from one another and are reported as two figures and never as one: the weekday half is 0 mismatches over ten datelines, the day-of-month half is 0 over ten, and the market-morning claim is 0 over ten, and the market mornings are 1237, 1242, 1258, 1263, 1270, 1279 and 1284, seven of them.** **Two figures in the prompt for day 1284 do not reproduce on derivation and the pages follow the derivation: the prompt prints the nineteenth of Greenmonth YR 319 and the pages print the nineteenth of Mudmonth YR 319, because Mudmonth 1 is day 1266 and Greenmonth 1 is day 1296; and the prompt prints a Monday and not a market day and the pages print a Thursday and a market day, because 1284 minus 1237 is forty-seven days and forty-seven leaves five, and a Saturday plus five is a Thursday, and a Thursday is a market day.** The prompt's own rule governs: a figure in the prompt that does not reproduce is the error and not the count. **The prompt's §8 describes the last page as a Monday; the last page is a Thursday, and that is the only place where this block and its instructions differ, and it differs because the weekday was derived and the instruction was not.**

1158. **The two dateline frames, and which chapters are in which.** **Chapters 473, 475, 476, 479 and 480 are set on the foreshore and carry a main-world date**, which is the precedent of Chapters 458, 468, 469 and 470 and not a lapse. Chapters 471, 472, 474, 477 and 478 are in the main-world frame away from the coast. **No chapter in this block is in the coast frame, and the coast frame of Chapters 466 and 467 is not broken and not referred to.** **No month name and no weekday is spoken in a character's mouth anywhere in the ten chapters**, and every month and weekday token in all ten files is in the narrator's dateline, and the count is 3, 4, 3, 4, 6, 4, 3, 9, 4 and 13 respectively, none of them inside a quoted span. **A yard keeps its count in days and a road keeps its count in tides and a path community keeps its count in crossings open and weeks shut, and no chapter of this block adds any two of the three.**

1159. **The block's finding, in the form it is on the page, and it is arithmetic and not a revelation.** **A cart-load of shell is six pence in the only book in the trade and nine pence in the mouths of three ports, and the difference between the two is three pence, and nine cart-loads a day on about two hundred days a year at three pence is five thousand four hundred pence a year.** **A widow at the eleventh house lives at twenty pence a day, and two hundred and seventy days is nine months, and two hundred and seventy days at twenty pence a day is five thousand four hundred pence.** So the whole of what a permanent connection would take out of that one household is nine months of her winter, and it would be taken by a page and not by a man with a chain. **The same five thousand four hundred pence is also the annual shortfall of a salt wagon that costs fifty-four pence a day and earns twenty-seven, and neither man has ever seen the other copy of the number, and the block does not have anybody put the two together.** A fixed price is a book; a book is a person who answers for it; that is all a keeper is. `state/open-threads.md` 160.

1160. **The money, read on the page and not from a state file, ten columns and ten footed totals.** **Ten columns, ten foots, ten distinct totals at 98, 106, 54, 87, 105, 72, 103, 122, 82 and 96 pence, in Chapters 471 to 480 in order**, and each column was added in pence and the sum list is printed before the total and the total after it. The ten owners are distinct: a man of about forty-four who is a salt factor, the man of about twenty-nine twice, the man of about fifty-three, the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow, the man of about thirty-four with a sheet, the man of about nineteen at a bar, the woman of twenty-nine, Aren Kest, and the man of about twenty-three who rows one of the four boats. **A mark is forty-eight pence in every conversion on the page without exception: 98 is a mark and fifty over, 106 is two marks and ten over, 54 is a mark and six over, 87 is a mark and thirty-nine over, 105 is two marks and nine over, 72 is a mark and twenty-four over, 103 is two marks and seven over, 122 is two marks and twenty-six over, 82 is a mark and thirty-four over, and 96 is two marks exactly.** None of the ten is one of the sixty-four printed columns already spent, which are Volume 09 Batches 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 at 16, 22, 17, 3, 112, 135, 70, 31, 56, 70, 40, 33, 29, 38, 42, 45, 48, 47, 144, 34, 37, 50, 44, 59, 46, 43, 78, 64, 68, 69, 30, 53, 39, 63, 65, 71, 97, 75, 88 and 89, and Volume 10 Batch 0001 at 24, 27, 32, 35, 41, 51, 57, 60, 67 and 77, and Batch 0002 at 23, 26, 28, 49, 79, 19, 55, 73, 83 and 62. **The forty recurring totals in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are not printed anywhere in this repository and this phase did not re-read them; the check was made against the sixty-four printed columns and not against the forty, and that is the weaker check and is stated rather than implied.** The four barred numerals are at zero across the ten files and no line item in any column prints one of them. **The one required conversion in the block is made once on the page, in Chapter 473: he is paid three sacks a trip, the ninth house has paid nine pence a sack for eleven years, and a trip is twenty-seven pence. The salt is not in the money column and the money is not in the salt, and no two prices of the same thing stand in any column together, and the corridor's rate is named once as a rate that is not a rate anybody set and is put in no column, and the reason it works is that nobody can check it.** No rate is printed for the tide road and no toll is taken on it and nothing in this block is a rate.
