1166. **The three craft checks, and what each returned, and they are the checks no instrument in this project has.** **(a) The protagonist's name on the page: `Aren` 7 and `Kest` 7 across the ten new files, reported separately, in Chapters 472, 477, 479 and 480, and 479 is a chapter about his own arithmetic and he is named four times in it in the narration, in a speech attribution and in the closing frame.** Zero would have been a failure. **(b) Quoted speech: 13, 7, 9, 6, 6, 9, 13, 10, 15 and 6 quoted spans in Chapters 471 to 480, and no chapter of this block is a chapter in which nobody speaks.** **The figures this item carried before the review repair, 12 and 14 for Chapters 471 and 479, did not reproduce; the recount is 13 and 15 and the difference is Chapter 479's now-printed speech.** Two chapters in a first printing had a conversation reported instead of printed — the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow's question to the man of about fifty-three in 473, and a two-turn exchange in 479 — and the block's repair pass replaced the first and one of the two in 479 and left a full reported paragraph standing in §Three of 479, which the review found and which item 1173 prints. **The block's certificate said both were printed and it was checked against the intention and not against the paragraph.** **(c) The `about` sweep, read and not counted: twenty-one hedges were cut from figures a person in the scene could have counted** — nine people, four people, nine seconds, four seconds and four of the eleven — and the rate came from 21.42 to 19.50 per 1,000, and the survivors are the age house form and the honest-uncertainty register, and a person standing in that room could not count any of them.

1167. **What did not move, and the whole of the list.** The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened and is not scheduled and nobody was sent and the word *failed* is used of nothing. The offer is unanswered and is not answered with a figure. About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list and this block does not put them on anything. The two villages are not back on a road. The corridor's rule is bad and its rate is not printed as a figure. The hearing stands and the quarantine instrument stands. The city is on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero. The valley is unreachable. The register is at seven questions and no findings, the day-book is at nine, and the count on the page under the fourth step is at nine, and the bench's nine things in about nineteen years is a fourth nine and none of the four is added to another or to a figure of persons. The three bars are three objects and this block touches none of them and carries no figure for the bar four miles west. Hushwater was not taken and neither of its two figures was printed. The press was not dated, the bell was not rung, no oath-stone was renewed, no cut was made and none is scheduled. **Stage 4 is half-spent and the qualification is not earned and not claimed, and a sweep of the ten new files returns zero for *stage*, zero for *ledger* and zero for *qualification*, and no chapter in this block says the ledger does or does not move, and a System panel is printed nowhere in this block and nowhere in this volume.** **The antagonist is not named and is not on a page, and nothing in this block revives the Volume 08 cooperation and nothing in it is a reconciliation or a reunion.** The unattributed two-line statement is at four of four and no fifth is printed.

1168. **The three counting systems, and the three separate figures of about nine hundred, and the two nines that are never added.** **A yard keeps its count in days, a road keeps its count in tides, and a path community keeps its count in crossings open and weeks shut, and no chapter of this block adds any two of the three.** **About nine hundred people on the sand and about nine hundred people paid twice in seven days in a weigh-house room four days up a made road are the same number and are not the same people and are never added**, and that is the whole of Chapter 475's finding and the whole of Chapter 471's second section. **About nine hundred sheets a month and about a hundred and four in one band on a fixed day and about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month are three counts of three different series and are not added and are not converted into one another**, and all three stand together on the page once, in Chapter 478, and are not summed there. About eleven hundred people are a fourth figure and are on nothing.

1169. **The wall, the man with the sheet, the second man, and the form, and exactly where each stands after this block.** **The wall of eleven paces and about two feet high at a hundred paces from the landward end, with a second footing course nine inches lower and about four inches of sand banked against its far side in four months, took the water over it twice on the twenty-eighth of Thawmonth exactly as it has twice a day since the four days it took to lay, and nothing about it is scheduled and no surveyor is on that sand in this block.** **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet has been on that sand five times since the middle of a month in Wolfmonth and there is nothing in the five of them that says why he comes when he comes, and nobody has asked him and he has not said.** **On the fifth time there was a second man, about twenty-eight, carrying nothing, with no chain and no rod and no sheet, who did not stand at the stone, who looked at the sand and then at the eleven houses one at a time, and whom four people noticed without any of them knowing they were noticing; and nothing in this volume says who sent him and nothing in this volume says whether he and the four men with a crowbar are connected.** **The form's fourth head is empty, nobody filled it in, nobody says that anybody has filled it in somewhere else, and the one time it is mentioned in this block it is mentioned as empty, by the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow asking whether it is still empty and by Aren Kest saying that it is.** Nobody has taken up the offer of one sentence about the wall.

1170. **The two wants, the six answers, the four clauses, and the ninth word, and where each stands after this block.** **Neither woman on that foreshore restated her want in this block and neither was thanked.** The woman of about fifty-one is referred to four times and is not on a page; the woman of sixty-three acts twice, puts a third armful of wood on her fire in Chapter 480 and boils water in Chapter 475, and says her want nothing, and has not been thanked. **The six answers are exactly as Chapters 468 and 469 left them and this block improves on none of them and turns none of them into a worse one; the sixth, that eleven houses are somebody's job, is the only instrument this block found working and it is on the page in Chapter 475 and Chapter 480 and nowhere else.** **The four clauses about a wagon are four clauses and not a rate and nobody has made a keeper of them, and the three rooms have not met again and no fixed day is fixed.** **The ninth word was said out loud nine times in a market town of about two thousand people in Chapter 477, and it was not said in any other chapter of this block, and nobody wrote it down, nobody un-said it, and nobody regretted it in a speech, and the saying of it opened nothing and the gate at the end of the nine-mile road is not opened and nobody was sent to it and the two roads were not compared on a page.**

1171. **The two men, the accent, and the four objects of the protagonist's interiority, and where each stands after this block.** **The breach between the two men is open and is not repaired in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology, nobody walks a road with anybody for pleasure anywhere in this block, and the word for what the two of them are is printed nowhere in it and the barred kin-term is at 2 and spent. Neither of the two men is on a page of this block and the span built over nineteen mornings is not reported as a repair.** **The accent is not played a fifth time and is not played in this block at all, and no kitchen is placed or displaced, and nobody says one word to him about any of it.** A notebook with eleven lines and a word on the eleventh and no twelfth line, a road-marked left palm warm at about the fourth hour, a sheet of nine inches by six with three lines and no head in the inside pocket of his coat, and two unreconciled numbers in his coat are all untouched and unmentioned in this block, and in Chapter 479 the sheet is referred to as a thing on a piece of paper with a fourth head on it and is not shown and not asked for. **He conducted nothing in this block, got nothing back, overrules nobody, and is not the reason anything on that foreshore works; the volume's season's budget of about three was spent in Block 0001 and this block used zero of the two crossings the outline allows it, and that is a decision and not an oversight and is recorded here because the choice was between overrunning the season and forbidding the block its two and the block took neither.** In Chapter 480 he carries one armful of wood and is thanked for it by one woman and to nobody else.

1172. **And the standing lesson this block hands on.** **A check that only compares two places cannot see a novel, and this block's own instruments reported it clean while three of its chapters were reporting a conversation instead of printing one, which is the fourth time that has happened in this project and the second time a reviewer found it rather than a script.** The second finding is smaller and is the same shape: **a figure printed in words can be a barred numeral and a speech-length certification can be the thing that prints it, and a block that writes its speech figures after its prose rather than before it will put sixty-six and fifty-two and thirty-six on a page in a book where those three are prohibited figures, and no sweep for a barred token finds them unless the sweep tests speech figures too.** The third is the one the successor will meet first: **the prompt for this block gave a day number that is right and two attributes of that day number that are wrong, and the attributes were internally consistent with each other, so a writer who believed the weekday believed the month, and a writer who believed the month would have footed every date in the block against the others and been wrong everywhere at once.** Derive both halves, separately, and report them separately.

## Volume 10, Batch 0003, review repair pass — items 1173 to 1182, and the highest number in this file is now 1182

**A review of this block read for craft, after the block had run all ten of its own checks and reported thirty-one repairs and a clean bill, found nine sites in three classes, and a recount made in the course of applying them found a tenth that the review had certified clean. This pass fixes them. Every figure named here was counted on the ten chapter files as they stand after this pass and the basis is named in each item. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is withdrawn, and items 1161, 1163, 1164 and 1166 have been corrected in place because they carried figures this pass moved. `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and was not read for repair and not written.**

1173. **The retired reporting frame was converted into its mirror image and not removed, twenty-six times, and the block's sweep could not see it.** `workspace/volume-10/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` §3 item 2 retires *Have said since that*, *Have worked out since that* and *Have been saying since that* with a hard stop and says in terms **Do not convert one form into another form.** The ten new files contain **zero** of the four-word form with *that* in it and **twenty-six** of the same construction with *then* in the place of *that*, in four shapes: *have said since then that*, *said since then that*, *has said since then that* and *have worked out since then*. **By file: 2 in 471, 4 in 472, 4 in 473, 3 in 474, 1 in 475, 2 in 476, 4 in 477, 1 in 478 and 0 in 479 and 480, and one of the twenty-six, in Chapter 474, is inside a printed speech and not in the narrator's frame.** The block's §7 item 7 tests *have said since that* and returns zero and certifies a clean block. **All twenty-six are removed**, and the sixteen that the review's own marker did not list, which are the past-tense shapes, were the majority of the class. The `about` count fell by three as a side effect, from 332 to 329, and two of the three were hedges in front of a countable figure and one was a hedge in front of the wall's purpose, and none of the three was a hedge the world is honestly vague about.

1174. **The retirement is forward-only and no state file said so, and a block-local zero was being read as a manuscript one.** On the four-word form *have said since that* the manuscript stands at **six hundred and seventy**: 13 in Volume 04, 272 in Volume 05, 132 in Volume 06, 142 in Volume 07, 110 in Volume 08, 0 in Volumes 01, 02, 03 and 09, and **1 at `chapter-0460.md` in this volume**, which is Batch 0001's and is not repaired here on the same principle item 1156 sets out. **None of the six hundred and seventy is in scope for a repair pass, and a sweep over ten files returns a block-local number and every number in this item is now reported with its scope.** The forward-only rule is now written into `workspace/volume-10/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` §3 item 2 and §7 item 7.

1175. **A chapter still reported a conversation the block's own certificate said it had printed, and the certificate was written after the edit and not read against the file.** `chapter-0479.md` §Three opened **What he said was that he had said a sentence about eleven paces of stone** and reported five clauses of a reply, of which the printed speech that followed carried one, and the content the report carried and the speech did not — sixty-four words, a fourth head, four days of counting, nine days of not thinking about it — was lost to the reader. **It is now printed as two turns of fifty and sixty-two, the first in two spans of six and forty-four, and the certification is footed on what is printed.** The block's repair pass fixed the fourth section of the same chapter and the Chapter 473 exchange, so the certificate's *two chapters* was true and its *both were replaced* was premature, and the two facts were nine days apart in the same file. No plot, date, money column or name is touched by this.

1176. **Four figures of persons and one figure of a duration did not reconcile against the scene, and all five were found by reading the object and not the chapter.** **Chapter 477 §Three** described *four of the other six* out of nine when the man of about thirty-three with the barrow is one of the nine and is the one the man of about twenty-nine says it last of the nine to, and the four described are four of the nine: the man of about forty in §One, the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow, the boy of about eleven and the woman who sent him, the carter of about fifty, and the man of about twenty-nine, with four of the nine undescribed. **Chapter 477 §Three** also printed *a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow* and, four lines later, *a man of about thirty-three with a barrow who had not heard it before*, and is now *a man of about thirty-three with a barrow and a boy sitting up in it*, which is a second person and not a drift. **Chapter 476 §One** claimed the second man was the first person in about nineteen years to stand on that sand who was not there to buy, to sell, to carry, to boil or to count a tide, four lines after the man of about thirty-four had done none of those five things there for the fifth time, and is now one of the two of them being there for the stone and the other one not being there for the stone, which is the version the chapter's own closing frame already used. **Chapter 476 §Three** had a woman of about twenty-seven thinking about something for nine days inside the chapter in which she noticed it, and the nine days now stand only in the chapter's closing frame, where a frame may look forward, and §Three says only that she has not worked out what she noticed.

1177. **The protagonist's residence did not survive from one chapter to another, and the block never accounted for a departure.** Chapter 472, the seventh of Thawmonth, has him *been in it five*; Chapter 477, the fifth of Mudmonth and twenty-eight days later, has him *been in that town nine days*. **He is now five days in that town at the start of Thawmonth and nineteen days out of it since, and it is his ninth day back in it on the fifth of Mudmonth**, which foots: the seventh of Thawmonth is 1242, the fifth of Mudmonth is 1270, and 1270 minus nine days is the twenty-sixth of Thawmonth. **The same dateline's other duration is also corrected**: a man of about twenty-nine who came up a made road four days and arrived at the eleventh hour of the second of Thawmonth in Chapter 471 has been *four days* in that town on the seventh and is now **five**, and the three pence that had been *sitting in him for two days* is now *sitting in him since the fourth day*, because two days and four days cannot both be true of the same gap. **A duration is a figure of a person and §7 item 8 of the Batch 0004 prompt now names it.**

1178. **One certification printed a turn's spans and not its total, and the total is a barred numeral.** `chapter-0478.md` §Two certified a hundred and thirty-two words in three turns, of which the first is sixty-four, the second is seven, and *the third is printed in two spans of six and fifty-five*, which foots at 64 + 7 + 61 = 132 and is unverifiable at turn level. **The third turn is sixty-one, and `sixty-one` is one of the four barred numerals, so the certification now states it as the rest of the hundred and thirty-two**, and a reader can add the two spans to the first two turns and get the whole. The block's own history is the reason: item 1161 records that a turn of sixty-six and a total of a hundred and thirty-three were re-footed in this chapter in the first printing for exactly this reason.

1179. **And a tenth site, found by re-counting what the review had certified clean, which is the whole argument for re-counting a certificate.** `chapter-0473.md` §Four certified *sixty-three words in two turns of one exchange, of which the first is six and the second is fifty-four printed in two spans of nine and forty-five*, and its own closing clause said he *has said fifty-four words out loud*. **The question is six words, the reply is nine and fifty-four, so the reply is sixty-three and the exchange is sixty-nine, and the printed figures were the turn total in the exchange slot, a span total in the turn slot and a stale span count in the span slot.** It now reads sixty-nine, of which the first is six and the second is sixty-three printed in two spans of nine and fifty-four. A review that hand-counted fifteen of this block's sixteen speech figures did not see it because the certification was internally plausible and no instrument in this project compares a certification against the speech it certifies. **All twenty-five printed figures have now been counted from the printed speech, turn by turn, and all twenty-five foot.**

1180. **The two prompts carried headline figures that did not reproduce, and one of them carried a right day number with two wrong attributes.** `workspace/volume-10/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` §0 printed **1,419,702** for four hundred and seventy chapters and the count on the files is **1,420,010**; §0 of `workspace/volume-10/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` printed **1,437,036** for four hundred and eighty, and the count as the block left it was **1,437,142** and as this pass leaves it is **1,437,152**. **Both are now the count, and the Batch 0003 §0 line names the figure it replaced**, because a count that has to be corrected by the phase that inherits it is a count nobody can check including the phase that wrote it. **The Batch 0003 §2 anchor for day 1284 printed the nineteenth of Greenmonth and a Monday and not a market day, where 1284 is the nineteenth of Mudmonth and a Thursday and a market day: 1284 is nineteen days after 1266 and Mudmonth is the month 1266 opens, and 1284 minus 1237 is forty-seven days and forty-seven leaves five, and a Saturday plus five is a Thursday.** The two wrong attributes agreed with each other, because a Monday is not a market day, so a writer who believed the weekday believed the month. **`chapter-0480.md` printed it correctly and the prompt was wrong, and the prompt now says so in bold in its own line with the derivation, so that a successor auditing 0480 against it finds the two in agreement instead of reading the chapter as the error.** Items 1157 and 1172 already recorded the derivation and the override; what was missing was the correction in the file that carried the error.

1181. **The self-reference sweep in the successor prompt tested one spelling of a retired rule, and that is the whole finding of this pass in one line.** `workspace/volume-10/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` §7 item 7 tested *have said since that* and returned zero, and the block that handed it the prompt had written the mirror image twenty-six times. **The item now tests both spellings, §3 item 2 names the mirror as the same retired frame and lists the four shapes the block wrote, and every number either item returns is required to be reported with its scope.** The general form, and it is worth more than the twenty-six: **a rule that names one form of a thing and not its mirror is a rule that can be obeyed by doing the opposite of it, and a sweep is a rule about a form, so a sweep on one form certifies a book that has written the other.**

1182. **What this pass did not do, and the standing lesson it hands on.** **It did not touch Volumes 01 to 09 and did not touch `chapter-0460.md`, so the six hundred and seventy of item 1174 are all still there and that is four volumes of a rewrite and not a repair pass.** It changed no date, no money column and no total, and the ten columns still foot at 98, 106, 54, 87, 105, 72, 103, 122, 82 and 96; it spent no name and the arithmetic stands at 3 + 1 + 0 = 4 of 5 with one slot left for Blocks 0004 and 0005; it did not fill the fourth head, did not name the antagonist, printed no third of the barred kin-term, printed the word that names the founding paper nowhere, and printed no System panel and no stage word. **The lesson, and it is the fourth block running that a certificate is a last place a defect survives: everything downstream reads the certificate and not the chapters, so a certificate written after an edit and not read against the paragraph is a worse place for a defect than the paragraph would have been.** `Aren` and `Kest` remain at 7 and 7 across the ten files and the name sweep still returns `Aren Kest` and `Tobin Kernick` and nothing else.

## Volume 10, Batch 0004 — items 1183 to 1206, and the highest number in this file is now 1206

**This section is written by the phase that wrote Volume 10 Batch 0004 and it continues from 1182. Every figure was counted on the ten chapter files as they stand after this block's last edit, and the basis is named in each item. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is withdrawn. The whole of this section is the fourth of five for the volume and the successor inherits all of it. This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, prints no System panel and no stage word, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

1183. **The frame, derived twice from the single anchor of day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month, and the two prompt figures that did not reproduce.** Day 930 is the only anchor. **Block 0004 runs days 1285 to 1332 and the ten chapter days chosen inside it are 1285, 1291, 1293, 1300, 1306, 1312, 1318, 1324, 1326 and 1326**, that is the twentieth, twenty-sixth and twenty-eighth of Mudmonth YR 319, the fifth, eleventh, seventeenth, twenty-third and twenty-ninth of Greenmonth YR 319, and the first of Longlight YR 319 twice, and they are a Friday, a Thursday, a Saturday, a Saturday, a Friday, a Thursday, a Wednesday, a Tuesday, a Thursday and a Thursday. **The weekday half and the day-of-month half were derived separately from the day number and not from one another and are reported as two figures: the weekday half is 0 mismatches over the eight main-world datelines and the day-of-month half is 0 over eight, and the market-morning claim is 0 over eight, of which five are market mornings and three are not.** The chain reproduces every anchor the plan prints: 930 is Mudmonth 25 and a Sunday, 1141 is Wolfmonth 26 and a Monday, 1146 is Frostmonth 1 and a Saturday, 1236 is Thawmonth 1 YR 319 and a Friday, 1266 is Mudmonth 1 and a Sunday, 1296 is Greenmonth 1 and a Tuesday, 1326 is Longlight 1 and a Thursday, 1356 is Rainmonth 1 and a Saturday, 1380 is Rainmonth 25 and a Tuesday. **Two figures in the prompt's §8 last-page sentence do not reproduce on derivation and the pages follow the derivation. The prompt prints *a Thursday in the nineteenth day of a month that is about to turn, the first of Longlight*, and no day inside 1285 to 1332 is both a Thursday and the nineteenth of a month: the Thursdays in the range are 1291, 1298, 1305, 1312, 1319 and 1326 and their days of month are twenty-six, three, ten, seventeen, twenty-four and one, and the nineteenth of a month in the range is 1314, the nineteenth of Greenmonth, which is a Saturday. The nineteenth of Mudmonth is 1284, a Thursday, and that is Chapter 0480's day and the last day of Batch 0003.** What reproduces and what the pages carry is that the last page is a Thursday and is the first of a month carried at thirty, and the block's last two chapters are that same Thursday at different hours. A second prompt figure did not reproduce: **§4's opening says seventy-four footed columns are spent and prints seventy of them, forty from Volume 09 and ten from each of this volume's first three batches, and the check in this block was made against the seventy printed, which is sixty-nine distinct values, and not against the forty recurring totals in `state/open-threads.md` item 36, which are printed nowhere in this repository.** The forty are stronger than the seventy and a successor with the item in front of it should make the stronger check and say which it made.

1184. **The two dateline frames, and which chapters are in which.** **Chapters 484 and 487 are in the coast frame** and carry an ebb and an hour and no month, no day of month and no weekday, and their main-world days are **1300 and 1318** and those numbers are here and not in the datelines. **The other eight are in the main-world frame and six of the eight are set on the foreshore.** **No month name and no weekday is spoken in a character's mouth in any of the ten chapters**, and a sweep of all ten quoted spans for the twelve month names and the seven weekday names returns one hit in a first printing, *Longlight* in the mouth of a woman of about twenty-seven with a basket in Chapter 489, which is now *a month*, and it is removed. A yard keeps its count in days and a road keeps its count in tides and a path community keeps its count in crossings open and weeks shut, and Chapter 487 prints all three of those and adds none of them, and no chapter of this block adds any two of the three.

1185. **The wall, exactly, and what four hundred yards is attached to, and what is not illegal about it.** **Eleven paces long and about two feet high at a hundred paces from the landward end, laid in four days out of a barge, with a second footing course nine inches lower, and about four inches of sand banked against its far side.** **In Chapter 485 a surveyor of about forty-four runs a chain from the landward face of the wall to the door of the eleventh of eleven houses and it comes to four hundred yards, and the chain goes slack in the middle twice, and four hundred yards is the only distance between the wall and any house that anybody has ever measured.** The prompt's §8 printed *about four hundred yards* for that distance and the pages print it without the hedge, because the surveyor chains it and the Batch 0003 craft repair lists four hundred yards among the figures that lose the hedge. The wall is not a boundary, not a toll, not a gate, not a rate and not a keeper, and it has not stopped a cart in the two months it has been there, and no man in nine counties has named a way it is against anything, and a surveyor of about forty-four could have named four ways of it in about four minutes the day he ran it and named none of them. **The fourth head of the form is empty, nobody filled it in, nobody says that anybody has filled it in anywhere else, and the only time the fourth head is argued about in this block is in Chapter 482, where a copyist of about thirty-one says it ought not to be on the form and is right about it and a clerk of about forty-seven says he has cut three of the four heads on about nine hundred forms in twenty-two years and has never cut that one, and neither of them is thanked and neither of them is a knave.**

1186. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet, the sixth time, and the question nobody asked, and the answer.** **He has been on that sand six times since the middle of a month in Wolfmonth and the sixth time is the seventeenth of Greenmonth YR 319.** On the fifth there was a second man, about twenty-eight, carrying nothing, who did not stand at the stone and looked at the eleven houses one at a time and whom four people followed with their eyes; **there was no second man on the sixth time and nobody looked for him, and nothing in this block says who sent him and nothing says whether the man with the sheet and the four men with a crowbar are connected, and nothing in this block names him and no person is put on the fourth head by anybody.** **The question nobody asked was asked in Chapter 486, in seven words, by a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow, and it was why he comes when he comes, and his answer is that he is sent, that a man in a room with a seal decides how often the place is looked at, that he has never been told who decided it and is not going to ask, and that the only thing about his six visits that is his own is that he has not found out whether the fourth head wants a person or wants a word.** The answer is not a conspiracy and it is not nothing and he is not a knave and he is not a liar. He writes four things on a sheet with no head on it and dates it at the top and he has never put a head on it. He is a return and not a name, and he is a Crown officer in nobody's mouth and nobody on that sand has ever asked him whether he is one.

1187. **The chain, and what it is for, and the boy of about seventeen with a rod.** **A chain answers one question and it is how far it is from the ring at this end to the ring at the other end along whatever the two men holding it walk over, and a chain run across one of the nine places the sand turns comes up with a longer answer than the ground under it.** The boy of about seventeen with a rod asks what a chain is for three times across Chapters 481, 484 and 485, and is told three times by three people, and the last telling is *a chain is a stick you lay on the ground to see how big the ground is; the ground is there either way.* **A chain and a rod are not rented for money on that shore and neither is in a money column in this block.** A boy of about seventeen with a rod is the surveyor's boy and he keeps his own column in Chapter 484, the first he has ever kept, on the back of a rod-measuring sheet with a pencil stub out of a shed. He is not one of the seven who refused a permanent connection in Volume 09 and he is not one of the nine and he is not a council and he is not given a name.

1188. **The ninth house, the nine weeks, and what it cost and who paid it.** **The ninth house did not buy for nine weeks and started again on the twenty-eighth of Mudmonth YR 319, buying three sacks at nine pence a sack, the price it has paid for eleven years.** A man with nine hundred acres inland said out loud that he did not stop buying because of the man with the sheet but because a wall of stone had appeared at a hundred paces from the landward end of a road with no line on it, and that a careful man does not put his salt on a road that has just been told about. **The two of them have an arrangement that is about nine weeks old and it is not on a docket with a nail on it, and the man of about fifty-three said the figure out loud to nobody and counted three sacks and stopped before the third.** **The price of the nine weeks is nine hundred pence, which is a sixth of five thousand four hundred pence, which is a wagon year at twenty-seven pence a day the wrong way on about two hundred days, and a sixth of it is forty-five days of the winter of a woman at the eleventh house at twenty pence a day.** None of those three figures is re-derived in this block, none is rounded, and the three sacks at nine pence a sack are not converted into money anywhere in this block, because that conversion was made once in Chapter 473 and the two prices of the same thing are never added in any register.

1189. **The two seasonal costs, and the rule that they are never added.** **A season on that sand is the days the water is off long enough, and a wagon year is about two hundred days, and the man of about fifty-three with a wagon is the only man on that shore who counts them out loud.** A widow of about seventy at the eleventh house lives at twenty pence a day, and about two hundred days at twenty pence a day is **four thousand pence**, and that is a new figure and it is the cost of a season to that household in money. **The cost of a season to the household at the ninth house is two carts up the made road, four days of a boy's legs each way, and one question asked out loud in front of nine people, and the man who paid it said himself that it is not pence and that he cannot give it to anybody in pence and that if anybody puts it in a column he will take the column out.** The two sit on the same stretch of sand in Chapter 488 and are not added, and **no rate in nine counties converts a boy's two legs up a made road into pence on that foreshore, and the corridor's rate is a rate nobody can check and this is not it, and no rate is printed for the tide road anywhere in this block.**

1190. **The two instruments, and that they do not touch.** **Four of the eleven houses are somebody's job and eleven paces of stone is at a hundred paces from the landward end, and in Chapter 487 both are on the same sand at the same hour with about nine hundred people crossing both of them, and the only conversation between the two is about a barrow between a man of about thirty-eight with a crowbar and a man of about thirty-three with a barrow.** **Four of eleven and seven of eleven are never added to eleven in a column, and about nine hundred people is a third figure and is not a figure of men, and the three are never added to find out what moves.** The four of them are the pan and the fire at the fourth house, the lease at the ninth, the widow with the last of a boat at the eleventh, and a man standing in the doorway of the seventh whom four of the wagon men will not stop at. **Nobody made a list. There is no list, there was no meeting, and there is no room on that sand.**

1191. **The two wants, the six answers, the four clauses, and the fever, and where each stands after this block.** **Neither woman on that foreshore restated her want in this block and neither was thanked, and neither want may be restated.** The woman of about fifty-one is referred to four times and is not on a page and is not thanked; the woman of sixty-three acts twice more, in Chapter 486 and Chapter 487, and says her want nothing. **The six answers are exactly as Chapters 468 and 469 left them and this block improved on none of them and turned none of them into a worse one**, and the sixth, that eleven houses are somebody's job, is the instrument this block put beside a wall and is on the page in Chapters 485, 487 and 490. **The four clauses about a wagon are four clauses and not a rate and nobody has made a keeper of them, and the three rooms have not met again and no fixed day is fixed.** **A fever is not resolved in this block, its source is not named, it is not used to argue for a keeper, and no character catches anything.** A fever sat four days in the seventh of the eleven in Batch 0003 and four people are still not named as the source of it and Batch 0005 may not name them either.

1192. **The keeper of records, and the figure of about eleven hundred, and the three counts.** **Mara Vey, twenty-nine, four years and a half, one room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane about two hundred miles off the coast, fourpence a year, out of a deed of thirteen words, is not on a page of this block and no chapter of this block is set in her room.** Her register stands at seven questions and no findings, her day's book at nine entries of a thing nobody asked, and her drawer about eleven inches deep is not opened. **About nine hundred sheets a month and about a hundred and four in one band on a fixed day and about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month are three series and are not added and are not converted into one another**, and Chapter 487 names all three counting systems and adds none of them. **About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list and this block does not put them on anything.** Nobody thanked her and this block did not thank her and did not thank her twice and did not restate her want and did not put her in a room with a man of thirty-two.

1193. **The protagonist, in the state Batch 0005 inherits him in.** **Stage 4, Convergence, half-spent; the qualification is not earned and not claimed.** A permanent limp in the right leg; a ferrier's stick he cannot lean on; he has not got up a bank since the seventh day of a month in Longlight and is not going to, and getting up is about four seconds longer than sitting down. A road-marked left palm, warm at about the fourth hour, still untold. A notebook with eleven lines and a word on the eleventh and no twelfth line. A sheet of nine inches by six with three lines and no head, in an inside pocket, not shown and not asked for. Two unreconciled numbers in his coat. **`Aren` 11 and `Kest` 11 across this block's ten files, in Chapters 481, 484, 485, 487, 488, 489 and 490, and he is at zero in Chapters 482 and 486 by design, because Chapter 482 is a room the man of about thirty-four with a sheet is in and Chapter 486 is that man's own chapter.** He conducted nothing, got nothing back, overrules nobody, and is not the reason anything on that foreshore works; in Chapter 481 the chain goes over a sand he is standing on and he is the man of about fifty-three's guest and not a party to it; in Chapter 484 the chain goes slack four feet from his boot and nobody says anything to him; in Chapter 489 he is the twentieth person over that sand on the first of a month and says so to nobody. **He conducted no crossing. The volume's season's budget of about three was spent whole in Block 0001, the outline allows Block 0004 one, Batch 0003 used none of its two, and this block used none of the one, so the volume has spent three of about three and Block 0005 has none, and that is a decision and not an oversight and it is recorded here because it is the fourth block running in which a writer chose not to overrun the season.** The accent is not played in this block and no kitchen is placed or displaced.

1194. **The two men, and the two bars, and the three nines, and the two counts, and what did not move.** The breach between the two men is open and is not repaired in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology, nobody walks a road with anybody for pleasure anywhere in this block, the word for what the two of them are is printed nowhere in it, and **the barred kin-term is at 2 across four hundred and ninety chapters and is spent and this block prints no third of it.** The span built over nineteen mornings is not reported as a repair and neither man of the two is on a page of this block. **The three bars are three objects and this block touches none of them and prints no figure for the bar four miles west of Marl.** The count on the page under the fourth step of an outside stair is at nine, the day-book is at nine, the register is at seven questions and no findings, and the bench's nine things in about nineteen years is a fourth nine, and none of the four is added to another or to a figure of persons. The unattributed two-line statement is at four of four and is spent and this block prints no fifth. The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened and is not scheduled and nobody was sent. The offer is unanswered and is not answered with a figure and nobody has taken up the offer of one sentence about the wall in the eighty-six days this block covers. About eleven hundred people are on nothing. The two villages are not back on a road. The hearing stands. The quarantine instrument stands. The city is on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero. The valley is unreachable. The press was not dated, the bell was not rung, no oath-stone was renewed, no cut was made and none is scheduled, and **nothing was opened in this block and nothing is scheduled to be.** The ninth word was not said a second time and the gate at the end of the nine-mile road was not opened and nobody was sent to it and the two roads were not compared on a page. Hushwater was not taken and neither of its two figures was printed. A System panel is printed nowhere in this block and nowhere in this volume.

1195. **The money, read on the page and not from a state file, ten columns and ten footed totals.** **Ten columns, ten foots, ten distinct totals at 91, 118, 76, 134, 85, 109, 143, 94, 127 and 100 pence, in Chapters 481 to 490 in order**, and each column is added in pence and the sum list is printed before the total and the total after it. The ten owners are distinct: a surveyor of about forty-four with a chain, a man of about thirty-four with a sheet, the man of about fifty-three with a wagon, the boy of about seventeen with a rod, the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow twice at 85 and 94 in two different chapters, the man of about thirty-three with a barrow, a woman of about twenty-seven with a basket, a man of about forty-one with a basket, and Aren Kest. **A mark is forty-eight pence in every conversion on the page without exception: 91 is a mark and forty-three over, 118 is two marks and twenty-two over, 76 is a mark and twenty-eight over, 134 is two marks and thirty-eight over, 85 is a mark and thirty-seven over, 109 is two marks and thirteen over, 143 is two marks and forty-seven over, 94 is a mark and forty-six over, 127 is two marks and thirty-one over, and 100 is two marks and four over.** None of the ten is one of the seventy printed columns, which are Volume 09 Batches 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 at 16, 22, 17, 3, 112, 135, 70, 31, 56, 70, 40, 33, 29, 38, 42, 45, 48, 47, 144, 34, 37, 50, 44, 59, 46, 43, 78, 64, 68, 69, 30, 53, 39, 63, 65, 71, 97, 75, 88 and 89, and Volume 10 Batch 0001 at 24, 27, 32, 35, 41, 51, 57, 60, 67 and 77, and Batch 0002 at 23, 26, 28, 49, 79, 19, 55, 73, 83 and 62, and Batch 0003 at 98, 106, 54, 87, 105, 72, 103, 122, 82 and 96. **The forty recurring totals in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are not printed anywhere in this repository and this phase did not re-read them; the check was made against the seventy printed columns and that is the weaker check and is stated rather than implied.** The four barred numerals are at zero across the ten files and no line item and no total in any column is one of them. **No rate is printed for the tide road, no toll is taken on it, and the two prices of shell are never added in any register including a notebook, and three sacks at nine pence a sack is not converted into money anywhere in this block.**

1196. **The speech-length figures this block prints, and how each was counted, on the declared basis of a whitespace token with punctuation stripped from its ends and a turn counted as a turn and not as a quoted span.** **Twenty certifications are printed and all twenty foot, and the word *about* precedes none of them, and every one is attached to speech the page prints.** Chapter 481 prints 139 in four turns of 25, 63, 6 and 45. Chapter 482 prints 97 in two turns of 10 and 87, 51 in one turn in two spans of 6 and 45, 122 in one turn, and 125 in four turns of 9, 46, 6 and 64, the last in two spans of 12 and the rest of the 64. Chapter 483 prints 87 in four turns of 2, 6, 10 and 69, 128 in three turns of 1, 1 and 126, and 158 in two turns of 91 and 67. Chapter 484 prints 123 in three turns **whose first two totals are 52 and 66, which are both barred numerals, and both are therefore given as their printed span sums with the reason stated on the page**, and 155 in three turns of 75, 6 and 74. Chapter 485 prints 38 in two turns of 5 and 33, and 45 in one turn in two spans of 10 and 35. Chapter 486 prints 137 in four turns of 7, 81, 4 and 45, and 111 in three turns of 70, 9 and 32. Chapter 487 prints 116 in four turns of 10, 67, 4 and 35. Chapter 488 prints 70 in two turns of 6 and 64, 140 in three turns of 7, 15 and 118, and 1 in one turn. Chapter 489 prints 125 in three turns of 45, 12 and 68, the last in two spans of 16 and the rest of the 68. Chapter 490 prints 74 in four turns of 3, 5, 18 and 48. **Seven of the twenty were wrong in the first printing and every one of them was wrong by drift after the `about` sweep cut a hedge out of a printed speech, which is item 1161's defect for the fourth time running, and the fix in all seven was to recount from the printed speech and not from the certification, and the fix in one of them was to change the prose instead of the figure because the drifted figure was right and the cut hedge was in the wrong place.** A speech-length figure is a figure and a barred numeral is barred in a figure, and this block printed three of them into certifications before the sweep found them: a turn total of thirty-six, a turn total of fifty-two, a turn total of sixty-six, and a span of fifty-two and a span of fifty-two in two other chapters. **This block adds no speech-length figure to the fourteen-unprinted-referent class and none to the list of protected unprinted figures, and no chapter of this block reports a conversation instead of printing one.**

1197. **The name budget with the arithmetic, and the sweep, and what this block did not spend.** The budget for Volume 10 is five. **Batch 0001 spent three, `Marda Oakes` 51, `Rusen Ball` 29 and `Halla Wray` 63. Batch 0002 spent one, `Tobin Kernick` 53, in `chapter-0468.md`. Batch 0003 spent none. Batch 0004 spent none, and the arithmetic is 3 + 1 + 0 + 0 = 4 of 5, so the last slot is untouched and it belongs to Batch 0005 whole.** The block's people are all reached by age and by trade, and a surveyor, a boy with a rod, a copyist, a clerk of the works, a man with a board and nine hundred acres, a widow of about seventy, a man of about forty-one who stands in a doorway, a man of about twenty-six with a shovel and a man of about thirty-eight with a crowbar are all descriptions and none of them is a name. **A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns `Aren Kest` and nothing else**, and every other hit is a sentence opening across a bold marker or a section heading and each was read before the sweep was reported. `Cray` was not re-swept in this block; `state/continuity.md` 1162 records the last sweep, which returns `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0254.md`.

1198. **The measurements, recorded because they are facts about the world and not because they are targets, and there is no target in this item for any of them. Every figure in this item was recounted after the Batch 0004 review repair and the figures this item originally carried are given in the correction at 1209, and the count that governs is the count on the files.** Block 0004 is **20,366 in ten chapters**, counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included and the ten added, and the per-chapter figures are **1,965 / 2,326 / 1,897 / 2,160 / 2,075 / 1,922 / 2,224 / 1,933 / 1,984 / 1,880**. **The four hundred and eighty chapters before this block are 1,437,152 on the same basis, recounted on the files and not read off any item, and it reproduces the figure `state/current.md` carried, and the manuscript is 1,457,518 in four hundred and ninety chapters, and the sum over the files is the authority and no concatenated stream was counted.** Volume 10 is 70,185 in forty chapters. `about` is **418 in 20,366, which is 20.52 per 1,000**, on the declared whole-word numerator over a whitespace denominator with a case-sensitive numerator, and both halves are not a figure to steer to. **Seventy hedges were cut from figures a person standing in that room could have counted**, and the survivors are the age house form and the honest-uncertainty register. `the whole of` is 15 and `about four people` is **0**. Question marks **4**, exclamation marks **4**, semicolons **6**, em dashes **1** and en dashes **0**, and there is no zero-punctuation mandate in this volume and none in this block. `---` rules **0**. Per-line bold parity is even on every line of every file. Curly quotation marks balance in every file and on every line. A doubled-word and space-before-punctuation sweep returns zero for both, and a missing-full-stop sweep returns zero.

1199. **The barred tokens, counted before they were enforced, and the denominators.** **Across the ten new files, whole word, case-insensitive, every hit read: `failed` `seam` `roof` `coping` `Gatehouse` `custodian` `melody` `Culling` `eastern` `hemp` `charter` `brother` are all at zero, and so are the four barred numerals `thirty-six` `fifty-two` `sixty-one` `sixty-six`, and `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` `protagonist` `council` and `hundredweight`, and the barred kin-term.** The barred kin-term is at 2 across four hundred and ninety chapters and is spent and this block prints no third of it. **A self-reference sweep across twenty-three patterns testing the bare noun returns 0 for every one of them across the ten files, and the sweep of the retired reporting frame now runs across nine forms and not six, and the three it gained are the two comma forms and the bare *said since that*, and it returns 0 for all nine. The block as first printed carried four instances of the frame in the two comma forms, at `chapter-0484.md`, `chapter-0486.md` twice and `chapter-0487.md`, and the six patterns this item ran could not see any of them, and a reviewer found them by reading the pages; all four are re-voiced and the correction is at 1209. A first printing of this block also carried five instances of the frame in the four-word form with *that* in it and three self-references, all removed by the block's own hand.** The retirement is forward-only: the four-word form stands at six hundred and seventy across the manuscript before this block and this block adds none, and none of the six hundred and seventy is in scope for this block and none is repaired, and **a zero returned by a ten-file sweep is a block-local zero and is reported as one.** The manuscript-scope figures this block inherits are not restated here and are in `state/current.md`; a successor counts before it enforces, because these are prohibitions written for two volumes against denominators of two and zero.

1200. **The duplication bases, their scopes, and what each returned, and the correction to this item, which is at 1209 and which every reader of this item must take with it.** **Raw token, headings stripped, bold datelines in, eight-word shingles, maximal-run basis: the ten new against all four hundred and eighty prior return a longest shared run of forty-six words, and it is the driftwood sentence in `chapter-0487.md` that stands whole in `chapter-0480.md` and carries the twenty-two notches; the dateline frame's own longest run inside the block is thirty-eight words, in `chapter-0483.md`; the ten new against themselves return a longest shared run of thirty-three words and it is the money-column frame in `chapter-0489.md`. On the looser six-word-shingle basis the same two figures are fifty-five and fifty-two and both are money columns.** The money-column frame, the dateline frame and the house footing sentence are declared and are not re-voiced as frames, and **a declared frame is not a licence to copy a column: four of this block's ten money columns were re-voiced in the review repair, in Chapters 484, 485, 486 and 487, with every price, every term and every total left exactly as they were, and the six that were not re-voiced are the six whose longest run is under forty words.** **Exact duplicate paragraphs, whitespace-normalised, twelve words or more: 0 for the ten new files against the whole manuscript and 0 against themselves, which is the correct result for the block, and the manuscript-scope figure this item carried was a scope-overclaim and is corrected at 1209.** One was found and repaired by the block's own hand, a four-heads paragraph in Chapter 482 that stood whole in `chapter-0458.md` from a Volume 09 hand. A block-boundary check on the dateline frame and the quotation-mark basis across blocks: clean, and one unclosed bold and one triple blank line in Chapter 482 were found by the sweep and closed.

1201. **The three craft checks, and what each returned, and they are the checks no instrument in this project has.** **(a) The protagonist's name on the page: `Aren` 11 and `Kest` 11, reported separately even at zero, in Chapters 481, 484, 485, 487, 488, 489 and 490.** A block in which he was on two pages would have been a failure, and he was on two in a first printing. **(b) Quoted speech: 6, 12, 13, 9, 5, 10, 6, 7, 5 and 5 quoted spans in Chapters 481 to 490, and no chapter of this block is a chapter in which nobody speaks.** **(c) The `about` sweep, read and not counted: seventy hedges were cut from figures a person in the scene could have counted, and the rate came from 24.66 per 1,000 to 20.48 as the block left it and stands at 20.52 after the review repair, and the survivors are the age house form and the honest-uncertainty register, and a person standing in those scenes could not count any of them.** **And the fourth, and it is the cheapest craft defect in this project and it was found in this block's own first printing: Chapter 490 put a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow and a man of about thirty-three with a barrow in a two-turn exchange four lines apart, which is the exact pair Batch 0003's review found in Chapter 477, and it is now a man of about forty-one with a basket, and a figure of a person standing next to a figure of a person a reader cannot tell apart is a defect whether or not two people were meant.**

1202. **And what this block did not do, which is the standing instruction for Batch 0005 and not a list of omissions.** It did not name the antagonist and it did not say whose plan the wall is. It did not revive the Volume 08 cooperation and nothing in it is a reconciliation or a reunion. It did not repair the breach between the two men and it did not report the span as a repair and it did not put either of the two men on a page. It did not fill the fourth head and it did not have a character say that the fourth head has been filled in somewhere else, and it did not have anybody who is not a Crown officer put a name on anything. It did not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement. It did not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration, and it did not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose. It did not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and it did not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move. It did not print a word about a roof and it did not mention a tile on a coping. It did not say the ninth word a second time, soften the leak, un-say it, add a tenth line to the song, or have anybody regret it in a speech. It did not open the gate at the end of nine miles of road, send anybody to it, or compare that road with the tide road on a page. It did not resolve the shut road four hundred miles off, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine. It did not reopen the city, the valley, the press's opening day, the roadmark, the mechanism, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. It did not merge the three bars and it printed no figure for the bar four miles west of Marl. It did not take Hushwater and it printed neither of that thread's two figures. It did not add a tenth asking of the bench, a tenth not-asking, or an eighth register entry. It did not print the corridor's rate as a figure and it printed no rate for the tide road. It did not resolve the fever, name its source, use it to argue for a keeper, or have a character catch anything. It did not renew an oath-stone and it scheduled no second swearing and it sent nobody to look for the missing one. It did not spend the last of the five names. **It did not improve on the block's finding and it did not round it, and it did not have any character explain the cost of the two seasons in a sentence easier than the arithmetic, and it put four thousand pence on a page beside two carts and did not add them.**

1203. **The two figures of the block that a successor is most likely to get wrong, and where they are.** **Four hundred yards is the chained distance from the landward face of the wall to the door of the eleventh of eleven houses, and it is exactly four hundred yards and it carries no hedge, and it is not a distance from the wall to the fourth house and it is not a distance from the wall to the seaward end.** **Four thousand pence is a widow's season at the eleventh house, being about two hundred days at twenty pence a day, and it is not five thousand four hundred pence, which is nine months of her winter and the cost of a permanent connection, and the two are on the same page in Chapter 488 and are never added and a successor may not add them.**

1204. **What this block's own hand found and repaired before the final run, in eight classes, and the standing lesson, which is the twenty-fourth this project has carried it.** One exact duplicate paragraph against the whole manuscript. Six speech figures that drifted when the `about` sweep cut a hedge out of a printed speech. Two barred numerals in speech certifications, one a turn total and one a span. Three self-references and five instances of the retired frame, in the four-word form with *that* in it and not only in the mirror, which is the finding of item 1181 applied to this block's own prose and is the fourth time the same mirror has cost this project a block. One doubled word, one unclosed bold and one triple blank line. A protagonist named in two chapters who is now named in six. And one pair of figures of a person four lines apart in Chapter 490. **Nine of the ten classes in this block were visible to an instrument and one was not, and the one that was not is the pair of barrows, and the one that took longest to find is the speech figures, because the hedge cut is a correct edit and the figure is a second measurement of the prose and the second measurement is the one that goes stale.**

1205. **What the volume has now spent of itself, and what is left, in one paragraph, for the close.** **Three blocks of coast work and two of assembly, and the volume's argument is that the only instrument anybody found in five blocks is a set of eleven houses that somebody's job and that nobody keeps.** A wall of eleven paces at a hundred paces from the landward end is four hundred yards from a house with a widow in it and there is no paper joining them and nobody in nine counties has named a way the wall is against anything. A chain is a stick you lay on the ground to see how big the ground is. The fourth head of a form is empty and the only person in nine counties who has cut the other three says it ought not to be on the form and is right and is not agreed with. A man of about thirty-four with a sheet has been on that sand six times and does not know who sends him and is not going to ask. Nine hundred people crossed both instruments on one tide and stopped on neither. Two women on that sand have been asked what they want once each in twenty-two and forty-one years and neither has been thanked. **Nothing in the volume has been opened, nothing has been scheduled, no oath-stone has been renewed, no cut has been made, no page has been turned, the offer of one sentence about the wall has not been taken up in about four months, and the water did not go off the mark on the twenty-second notch and the twenty-second notch is not cut.**

1206. **And what Batch 0005 must not do, in one short list, because item 1202 is the long one and this is the one that came out of writing a block about a wall.** **Do not write the retired frame in either of its spellings, and do not write a near-miss of it, and do not certify a sweep that tested one spelling.** Do not report a conversation instead of printing one, and do not certify a repair without reading the paragraph it is in. Do not let a figure of a person stand next to another figure of a person a reader cannot tell it from, and do not let two barrows be in one exchange. Do not let a duration of a man's stay or a gap between two visits be different in two chapters without a departure on the page. Do not print a speech figure that is a turn's total in an exchange's slot, and do not print a barred numeral in a figure of any kind, and do not print a total a reader would have to subtract unless the total is a barred numeral, and then say that it is. Do not trust a prompt's day number, a prompt's week number, a prompt's headline count or a prompt's count of how many columns are spent, because all four of this block's did not reproduce. Do not cut a hedge out of a printed speech and leave the figure standing. Do not report a block-local zero as a manuscript zero. Do not vary four hundred yards or four thousand pence or any of the ten figures Batch 0003 added to the trade. Do not put a fifth unattributed two-line statement, a tenth asking of the bench, a tenth not-asking, or an eighth register entry on a page. Do not fill the fourth head, in speech or on paper, and do not have anybody accept the offer of one sentence about the wall. Do not print the word that names the founding paper. Do not name the antagonist and do not say whose plan the wall is. Do not resolve the fever, the offer, the shut road, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine, the Hushwater thread, the city, the valley, the roadmark, the mechanism, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. Do not reopen anything, schedule anything, open anything, or renew an oath-stone. Do not spend the last of the five names on a surveyor, a clerk, a copyist, a boy with a rod, a man with a crowbar, a man with a shovel, a man with a board, or a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow. Do not make any of the four men with a crowbar, a shovel, a sledge and a barrow a villain, a knave or a liar, and do not have one of them discover that he has been wrong about something and go away. Do not have the protagonist conduct anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, or be the reason a thing works, and do not have him laid a chain he is a party to. Do not print a rate for the tide road. And **do not end the volume on a summary of what the volume meant, and do not end it on a wall winning and do not end it on a wall coming down, and do not end it on a man standing on a road.**

## Volume 10, Batch 0004, review repair pass — items 1207 to 1212, and the highest number in this file is now 1212

**This section is written after a reviewer read the ten chapter files of Volume 10 Batch 0004 and the five state files that block wrote. It continues from 1206. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is withdrawn, and items 1198, 1199 and 1200 have been corrected in place because they carried figures that were false, and each of them now says so and points here. No chapter of new prose is in this section, the antagonist is not named in it, no third of the barred kin-term is in it, the word that names the founding paper is printed nowhere in it, and it prints no System panel and no stage word. The block is not restarted and its plot is not changed: what changed is four money columns that were copies of columns in earlier volumes, four clauses that were the retired reporting frame, two datelines, and five state-file sentences.**

1207. **The five findings, in the order a reader needs them, and which of them were in the pages and which were in the certificate.** **One, in the pages and in the certificate together: a money column in `chapter-0485.md` stood a hundred and twenty-one words against `chapter-0475.md` on a six-word-shingle basis and a hundred and four on the declared eight-word basis, and the certificate for that block reported the block's longest run as fifty-eight words and named the dateline frame.** **Two, in the certificate only: the claim of zero exact duplicate paragraphs against the whole manuscript was a scope-overclaim, and the manuscript figure is thirty-three paragraphs of twelve words or more standing whole in two or more different files, every one of them in Volumes 01 to 07, and three further files repeat a paragraph inside themselves.** **Three, in the pages: four instances of the retired reporting frame stood in two comma forms that none of the six sweep patterns was aimed at, at `chapter-0484.md`, `chapter-0486.md` twice and `chapter-0487.md`, and the certificate reported the frame at zero in both spellings as an assertion where a reading was required.** **Four, in the pages and in the successor prompt: the block's own prompt declared the money-column frame a motif, which is a licence to reuse it verbatim, and one hundred and twenty-one words of verbatim reuse was sitting in the block the prompt was handing on.** **Five, in a file no writer may touch: `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `currentPhase: phase-000-bootstrap` and `status: planned` and `attempts: 0` at chapter 490, and only two commits have ever touched it.**

1208. **What the repair changed in the ten files, and the rule it held to, which is that a re-voice may not move a fact.** **Four money columns were re-voiced, in Chapters 484, 485, 486 and 487, and in each of the four every price, every term, every printed sum-list entry, every total and every mark conversion is exactly what it was before: 134, 85, 109 and 143 pence, and the same four totals, and the same thirty-eight, twenty-six, thirty and forty-five terms in that same order of chapters.** The items were re-ordered and re-worded, not re-priced: `chapter-0484.md` moves to a boy's column that opens on bread and closes on tar, `chapter-0485.md` moves to a column that opens on a wheel for a barrow and puts the loaves last, `chapter-0486.md` opens on a room and `chapter-0487.md` on three sheets of paper and takes the fourth sheet away to the end. **Two datelines were re-voiced, the market-town clause in `chapter-0482.md` and the salt-wagon clause in `chapter-0483.md`, and both keep every figure they carried: about two thousand people, four days off a coast, a market on two days in seven, a street four hundred yards long, one window, six chairs, no fire, and a wagon nineteen years off and on that sand with the horses in the water.** **Four clauses were re-voiced, one in each of `chapter-0484.md`, `chapter-0486.md` and `chapter-0487.md`, and they are the four instances of the retired frame, and the facts they carry are unchanged: the man with the barrow gave one reason for answering the boy and it is the one on the page, four people on that sand judged the answer to be the smaller of the two things they expected, one of those four expected the larger, and four of nine say the man with the barrow was right while four say a wall and a boat are not the same size of thing.** **No quoted speech was touched in any of the ten files, which was checked by comparing every quoted span in the ten files against the ten files as the block left them, and all seventy-eight spans are byte-identical, and every one of the twenty printed speech-length certifications therefore still foots against the speech it is attached to without being recounted.**

1209. **The corrections to the block's own figures, with the figure that was wrong beside the figure that is right, because a correction nobody can check is a correction nobody made.** **Word counts, on the declared basis of a plain whitespace split with the title line in, each file on its own and the ten added: the block was 20,357 and is 20,366; the per-chapter figures were 1,965 / 2,326 / 1,896 / 2,155 / 2,072 / 1,916 / 2,230 / 1,933 / 1,984 / 1,880 and are 1,965 / 2,326 / 1,897 / 2,160 / 2,075 / 1,922 / 2,224 / 1,933 / 1,984 / 1,880; the manuscript was 1,457,509 and is 1,457,518; Volume 10 was 70,176 and is 70,185; the four hundred and eighty chapters before the block are 1,437,152 and did not move.** **`about` was 417 in 20,357 at 20.48 per 1,000 and is 418 in 20,366 at 20.52, and the one word is an *about* on an age that Chapter 0483's re-voiced dateline put back, and the block's length also went up by nine words.** **Duplication: the block's longest shared run against the four hundred and eighty prior was reported as fifty-eight words and was in fact 104 on the declared eight-word basis and 121 on the looser six-word basis, and both were the same money column in **After the repair the true figures are 46 against the prior and 33 between chapters of the block on the eight-word basis, and 55 and 52 on the six-word basis, and the 46 is the driftwood sentence that carries the twenty-two notches and stands whole in `chapter-0480.md`, and the 33 is the money-column frame in `chapter-0489.md`.** **Duplicate paragraphs: the claim was 0 against the whole manuscript and the figure is 33 groups in two or more different files, sixty-seven chapter instances of them, by volume 12 in Volume 01, 7 in Volume 02, 18 in Volume 03, 20 in Volume 04, 4 in Volume 05, 4 in Volume 06 and 2 in Volume 07, with three files repeating a paragraph inside themselves at `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0037.md`, `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0052.md` and `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0113.md`, and the largest clusters are the six-paragraph letter in `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0148.md` that stands whole in `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0153.md` and the writ in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0025.md` that stands whole in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0026.md`.** **None of the thirty-six involves a chapter of this block, and the block-local zero is correct, and the certificate was wrong in the scope of its claim and not in the work it had done.**

1210. **The retired reporting frame, in the nine forms it can be written in, and the shape of the hole in the sweep.** The six the block's prompt named are *have said since that*, *have worked out since that*, *have been saying since that*, *said since then that*, *has said since then that* and *have worked out since then*, and the three that were missing are **the two comma forms, *have said, since that, that* and *has said, since then, that*, and the bare *said since that***. **All nine return zero across the ten files after the repair, and the comma forms return three and one before it, and a comma in front of the adverbial does not make it a different frame, which is the whole of item 1181's lesson applied to a sweep that was written after the mirror and before the comma.** The four instances were at `chapter-0484.md` in the certification of the boy's second question, at `chapter-0486.md` in the section that judges the answer and in the paragraph about the nine cart-loads of shell, and at `chapter-0487.md` in the certification of the two instruments. **The retirement is forward-only and is unchanged: the four-word form stands at six hundred and seventy across the manuscript before this block, and this block adds none, and none of the six hundred and seventy is in scope here and none is repaired.**

1211. **What was not changed, and why, and the one thing a reader of this section may think should have been.** **No chapter of Volumes 01 to 09 was edited.** The thirty-three duplicate paragraphs and the six hundred and seventy of the retired frame are both inherited, both sit four hundred and ninety chapters back, and item 1182 recorded the same decision for the same reason two blocks ago: a pass that rewrites three hundred and thirty chapters to remove thirty-three paragraphs is not a repair pass, it is a different book. **The inherited defect is now written down with its locations and its denominators and it is item 204 in `state/open-threads.md`, and it is a whole-volume job with its own budget.** **The same scope overclaim is written in about twenty-four places across the four ledgers, in the entries of earlier blocks, and they are not rewritten here for the reason given above. Two of them are honest about it, at `state/batch-summaries.md` 93 and 219 and at `state/continuity.md` 226, where the sweep's manuscript total of thirty-two and thirty is printed beside the block's zero, and the rest are not, and a reader of any of them should take the manuscript figure as **33** and not as the zero that is printed.** **`state/phase-ledger.json` was not edited and is not edited here, and the discrepancy a reviewer found in it is real and is item 207 in `state/open-threads.md` and it is addressed to the controller and not to a writer.** **Nothing in the block's plot was changed, no date was changed, no money total was changed, no figure of a person was changed, no name was spent, the fourth head is still empty and the offer is still untaken and the wall still stays.**

1212. **The standing lesson, and it is the twenty-sixth this project has carried, and this pass is the first one where the defects were mostly in the certificate.** **Item 1204 recorded the same lesson from the other end, that a hedge is a correct edit and a speech figure is a second measurement of the prose, and this pass found that the block's own instrumentation was a second measurement of its own duplication and got it wrong by more than double, in the wrong direction, with the wrong classification, and that a scope claim of zero was written for a check that had only been run over ten files.** **A number a writer computes about his own block is the number most likely to be wrong, because it is the only number in the file that nobody else has computed, and a reviewer is a reader and not a machine, and the defect class a machine cannot see in a certificate is the class that is in the certificate.** **The two repairs this pass made to the block's own prose were both invisible to every sweep the block had run, and the two repairs it made to the block's own certificate were invisible to the block, and the cheapest rule available to a writer who cannot run a reviewer is to open the file that says the number is zero and look at the paragraph the number is about.**

## Volume 10, Batch 0005, the last block of the volume — items 1213 to 1225, and the highest number in this file is now 1225

**This section is written after the ten chapters that close Volume 10, days 1333 to 1380 YR 319, and it continues from 1212. Volume 10 is complete at Chapter 500 and no chapter of Volumes 01 to 09 was edited by this block.**

1213. **The refusal, in nine sentences, in a salt-store, on the twenty-first of Rainmonth, and the reason it is not a principle.** In `chapter-0496.md` a man of about thirty-four from the port four days off asks, in nine words, whether his road and the coast's history can be made to be one account, and Aren Kest says no in one word and is asked to say it again and says it again in the same word and then says why. **The nine reasons are 38, 15, 25, 62, 53, 53, 26, 32 and 32 words in that order, printed as nine sentences across nine turns with six two-word turns of *go on* between the first six of them and none between the last three, and the whole exchange is 444 words in eighteen turns including a 39-word preamble and a 52-word answer.** The reason is not a principle and the page says so twice: the coast's history is twenty-two notches cut in the edge of a piece of driftwood and the notches are years, and his own is a road with a beginning, and a road with a beginning is written in days, and putting the two in one account forces one of them to be a keeper, and a keeper is a person, and a person can be asked. **He says the no in the coast accent and knows he is saying it in that accent, and about four of the nine people in that room notice the voice and about five do not, and the two groups do not overlap at all.**

1214. **What the coast set, and when, and that it was four days before he would have set it.** The terms go on the coast's own paper on the seventeenth of Rainmonth, day 1372, in `chapter-0495.md`, **four days before the question is asked in the salt-store, and the man of thirty-four with a sheet had carried the sheet up a made road four times before that and had said nothing about it to anybody.** Three terms: the road is a road while the water is off it and a place with water on it while the water is on it; the eleven houses are the road's keeper and four of them are the four that keep it and seven are the way out of the weather; and the water is the only thing on that coast that decides anything. **His no is the second thing that happens and not the first, and he is not the reason the paper exists, and the woman who carried that paper did not know he was on the sand and he did not tell her and she did not ask.**

1215. **The fourth head, and it is a word and not a person, and the name budget is spent at four of five.** The coast's own sheet is a foot and a half long and has four heads cut into it: a place, a figure of houses, a trade, and a fourth head holding **a word, which is the coast's own name for the road and is not a keeper and is not a person.** The Crown's form in the market town four days up is a different piece of paper with a different head on it, has three heads filled and a fourth head empty, and **the twenty-second of a month carried at thirty is named on the page and the fourth head is empty on the page.** **The block spends no name: the arithmetic is 3 + 1 + 0 + 0 + 0 = 4 of 5, and a capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns `Aren Kest` and nothing else.**
