1279. **The figures of persons this block puts on the page, and that none of them is added to another.** **About two thousand people in the market town; about four hundred at the end of the made lane; the about four hundred that went under a second head of a return in Harvestmonth, which is the same figure and is not the same figure; about nine hundred on the sand four days off; about nine hundred sheets a month; about a hundred and four in one band; about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month; about four hundred people in that town; about nine people in the hired room; about nine hundred and about two thousand at the four-day road; four people at a rail of a common, six at the eleventh of eleven houses, and a man of about seventy in the fourth house; and the four hundred and eighty-odd implied nowhere. **A figure of a person standing four lines from another figure of a person is the class no instrument owns and Chapter 530 had one: the woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key is a different person from the woman of about thirty-four at the end of the made lane, and a first printing gave both of them a first cost column in the same volume; hers is her second, the first being Chapter 517's.** Those are counts of different things and this block does not add any two of them and does not convert one into another, **and the block's own money columns say so on the page in all ten of them, each in a different order and different words, and no two of those ten sentences make the same run against another.**

1280. **The measurements, with their basis named.** **Word count, plain whitespace split, title line included, each file on its own and the ten added: the block is 20,718 and the per-chapter list is 2,574 / 1,905 / 1,990 / 2,057 / 2,091 / 1,834 / 1,801 / 2,241 / 2,031 / 2,194.** The five hundred and twenty chapters before this block recount file by file on the same basis are **1,525,382** and the manuscript is **1,546,100 in five hundred and thirty chapters**, and **the 1,525,382 reproduces the figure at the Volume 11 Batch 0002 close exactly, so the inherited total is confirmed by recount and not replaced by it.** **The calendar, derived twice from day 930 and never from one half to the other: the day-of-month half is 0 mismatches over ten datelines, the weekday half is 0 over ten, and the market-morning claim is 0 over ten, of which five are market mornings.** The ten chapter days are 1489, 1493, 1496, 1500, 1503, 1508, 1513, 1521, 1529 and 1542, and the weekdays are Saturday, Wednesday, Saturday, Wednesday, Saturday, Thursday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday and Wednesday, and **no chapter of this block falls on a Sunday, so the Sunday rule and the unwashed floor are not touched.** **Ten footed money columns at 92, 99, 111, 117, 125, 137, 145, 151, 158 and 165 pence, ten distinct totals and ten distinct owners, 102 items in columns of 8 to 12, prices from 2 to 31 pence across 21 distinct values, every column verified verbatim against its own page and re-verified after the last prose edit, every mark conversion footed on the page, and none of the ten colliding with the hundred and ten spent columns; the forty recurring totals in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are printed nowhere in this repository and were not re-read, and that is the weaker check and is stated rather than implied. The longest run any column makes against any other column in this block is zero words.** **No speech-length figure is printed anywhere in the block; the one figure of that class is a turn count in Chapter 528 and it is footed on the page, and every figure of words attached to a document or a short utterance was counted from the sentence as printed.** **A sweep of the same shape returns thirty-nine matches for *N words* in the ten files, and every one of them is a figure about a document or a short utterance — a four-figure number cut into a head, four words cut into wood, nine words on a margin, nine words on a strip, nine words on a leaf, two words said to a copyist, two words said to a man on a lane — and not one of the thirty-nine is a certification of the length of a speech the page prints. The two figures are different objects and a reader who counts the thirty-nine against the zero above is reading a different number.** Punctuation as a count of marks actually used: **13 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 2 semicolons, 4 em dashes, 0 en dashes and 0 horizontal rules.** Per-line bold parity is even on every line of every file, the curly quotation marks balance at 173 and 173 across the block and at 16, 23, 11, 24, 22, 11, 15, 25, 15 and 11 per file and on every line, and the straight-quote count is 0. **`about` is 548 in 20,718, which is 26.45 per 1,000, on a case-insensitive whole-word numerator over a whitespace denominator, and 542 on a lower-case-only numerator, which is 26.16 per 1,000, and both are reported because the case convention is the whole difference between them and because Batch 0002's 26.41 is this block's figure on the case-insensitive convention and not a fall in the register, `the whole of` is 5, and a sweep for *about* standing in front of a figure of a thing somebody in the scene could have counted returns 0.** The barred tokens are at **0** across the ten files and so are the four barred numerals and the kin-term, a self-reference sweep across sixteen patterns returns **0** at block scope, and **a sweep for the eleven bare-noun self-references returns **202 at manuscript scope, every one of them in Volumes 01 to 07 and 09 and none in Volume 08 or Volume 10**, and the four-word reporting-frame family is a separate and larger count at **1,608, all of it in Volumes 04 to 08 and 10**, and the two are different objects and a number without its basis and its family beside it is a claim about a different book.** **`Aren` is at 20 and `Kest` is at 20 across the ten files, in nine of the ten chapters, reported separately.** The duplication, raw token, headings stripped, bold markers removed, eight-word shingles, maximal-run basis: **the ten new against all five hundred and twenty prior return a longest shared run of thirty-two words on the declared basis, thirty-three if punctuation is stripped, and it is a dateline frame shared with `chapter-0505.md`, and the ten new against themselves return twenty-seven words and it is the sentence at the foot of a return, which is the form's own printed instruction.** Exact duplicate paragraphs, whitespace-normalised, twelve words or more: **0 and 0**, recounted after this block's last edit, and **a zero for a block is not a zero for the book, because thirty paragraphs of twelve words or more stand whole in two or more files across the manuscript**, which is item 204 in `state/open-threads.md`. **The name budget stands at 2 of 5 and this block spent none.**

1281. **The repair pass over this block, recorded as canon because four of its eight findings were claims about what a person said, and a claim about a person is not a measurement and no instrument in this repository can read it.** Chapter 527's closing paragraph reported a conversation the chapter had already printed and four of its seven claims were false on the page; it now prints what the man of about thirty-four with a sheet did with the leaf. **Chapter 521 said nobody in that room had asked the protagonist for anything in four months, and Chapter 520, four days earlier, prints a man of about fifty-eight asking him what the room was for and his answering; the chapter now says the last time anybody asked him for anything was four days before, on a Friday, and that it was about what the room was for.** Chapter 528's dateline named three hours and the body staged four, and a sentence in the same chapter said one question had been asked of him on a day the page prints two. **Chapter 529 said the woman of about forty-four was the only person in nine counties who had handled the stone, and the same page prints a man carrying it off a boat and a woman on that sand wrapping it in oiled paper; the claim is now that she is the only person who has put a hand flat on it.** Chapter 530's inventory put three bare leaves in Hearthmonth when the last was made in Frostmonth, and its cost column called itself the first its owner had ever kept when Chapter 517 prints her first. **Chapter 524 said a sentence had been said twice in that room and Chapter 530 said once, and Chapters 505 and 514 make it three times, which is what both now say, and Chapter 530's speaker now asks for it to be minuted as the third and not the first.**

1282. **The measurements after the repair pass, recount on the ten files as they stand and not carried forward from the first printing.** The block is **20,718** words and the manuscript is **1,546,100** in five hundred and thirty chapters, on the same file-by-file plain whitespace basis, and the five hundred and twenty prior chapters recount to **1,525,382** on the same basis and reproduce the Batch 0002 close exactly. The longest maximal shared run against the prior manuscript is **32 words** on the declared basis and **27 words among the ten new**, and **a first printing of this block's own certification said thirty-eight for the first of those and the count does not reproduce on any of the four normalisation variants tried, which is the cheapest defect in this project and the second block in a row that has shipped one.** Exact duplicate paragraphs are **0 and 0** at twelve words or more. `about` is **548 in 20,718, 26.45 per 1,000**, case-insensitive, and **542, 26.16**, lower-case-only. **The lesson, and it is the same one this project has now paid for in three consecutive blocks: a figure computed once is not re-counted, a figure whose basis was not recorded is a figure nobody can check including its author, and a claim about what a person said, did, was asked, or believed is not a measurement at all — it is prose, and prose is checked by a person reading the page and by nothing else.**

## Volume 11, Batch 0004 — the highest number in this file after this section is 1293

1283. **The block's object, and it is a head and not a form.** On the tenth of Hearthmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day, a man of thirty-two with a limp carried a set of sheets into a hired room over a saddler's and said he had not got a name for them, and the man of about fifty-eight asked what the thing was to be called. The room went looking out loud, one person at a time, and found that there is no head in nine counties for a record of a man's own wrongdoing: a day-book has heads for a day, an hour, a person and a thing; parish rolls have heads for a parish and a year; a toll-house roll has a head for a gate; a muster has a head for a company; the bare leaves on that table have no heads at all. **The man of about fifty-three with a barrow said the reason: a man's own wrongdoing is a thing a man keeps to himself, or a thing a court keeps against him, and the first has no head because nobody wants to look at it and the second has a head with a name over it and the name is not his.** The man of about fifty-eight ruled one, which is the second head he has ruled in that room in eleven years, and the copyist of about twenty-six cut it with her knife and her straightedge at the head of the first page, and **ruled nothing under it**, and said out loud that if anybody in that room rules a line under it in her absence she will take the knife out of it. **The finding, which the man of about fifty-eight gave as a finding and which is not one: a record with a head over it can be shown to a person, and a record without one can only be produced by the man who made it, and that is the whole of what the head is for.** A head is a shape and it is not an order; a head is not a fourth head; a head is not on a form; and there is nothing under this one to put an answer in. Aren Kest wrote his own name in it in his own hand and it took him longer than writing a name takes a man with a good hand, and nobody offered him a better one and the room did not read it out.

1284. **The record, and what is on it, in the order the room got through it.** Four pages under the head, and the first of the four is not the city. **Page one is the command**, stated in the plainest terms the man can find: he was twenty-one, he was a captain by brevet, on the nineteenth of Mudmonth, six years ago and a little, at about the second hour of the night, a courier gave him a paper at a gate, and the paper named a crossing, a district and forty men, and gave him a term of about a day and a night, and did not tell him what the term was for, and he did not ask, and he has written both of those on the page because both are true and only one is a defence. He held it for about a day and a night and let about nine people over it who had not been told there was anything to be held, and he did not tell them either, and he did not send them back, and the plainest words on the page are *I did not send them back*. **The record does not say where the fever came from and will not, and the reason is on the page in his own words: he was the man standing on the crossing and cannot tell and will not guess, and a record that ends in a cause is a record that has found somebody, and the somebody is him.** Nobody in that room argued with it and nobody in that room was thanked for reading it. **Page two is a word.** The account of the crossing came up the made road on a cart with the forms and he signed for the cart, and the account gave a day and an hour and a figure and a cause, and the cause is the word *none* — and *none* is printed at the head of every form in nine counties, in letters smaller than the four heads, cut in the die with them, and it reads *Fever in this parish: none since the last return.* **That is a printed line and not a ruled line, and a printed line is not a fifth line and a fifth line is not a fourth head, and the man of about fifty-eight said so out loud.** The finding, spoken by the man of about thirty-four with a sheet: a man who has recorded the mistake he made with a piece of paper has to go and record the mistake he made with a word, and he cannot do the second one by writing more, because the second one was not a not-writing. **Page three is his mother's crossing and it is one page and it is the only page he read aloud himself.** It is a copy of a thing not in this county; on it is a sign made with a pen pressed hard that does not spell anything and is not a hand, and above the sign a day written out in full in his own hand of about six years ago. The page says what the thing is not and says he is not going to say what the sign is, because he does not know, **and because a record of what a man did is not an explanation of anything.** The four lines he read aloud: *My mother made a crossing. It was on the day written above and not on any day I can find out. I was not there. I have not asked her, and I have not said any of this out loud before.* **Nothing in that page is a crossing, nothing was conducted, nothing was cut, no mechanism is explained, no word about a roadmark is printed, and no chapter of the block says what a sign on paper does or why anybody can cross.** **Page four is a list of five things he does not know and is not going to be asked about in a room by anybody with a badge, and it has no head cut into the wood over it and is not in the room's book.**

1285. **The reading, and the cost of it, and the two readings of the man who published it.** On the twenty-ninth of Hearthmonth, a Thursday and a market day, the record was read out loud once in an upper room over a tallow chandler's hired for the day by a man who was not from the inquiry, and about four hundred people were in it, and **the office was not in the room for the first time in this volume**: the man of about fifty-eight sent word on the Wednesday with a reason in nine words, and the nine words are *this record is not what a badge can hold*, and about half the people in that room had never heard them. **A woman of forty-one called Hester Ollett read it out.** She reads at the chapel at the end of a made lane and at the market cross on Thursdays, and she asked what she was paid before she said yes, and she was paid, and nobody in that room thanked her. **About four hundred people who had come to hear a thing about a form were told a thing about a man, and it was not a shock: it was a change of subject.** Nobody filled anything in; there was no form in the room, no clerk, no press, no die and no badge. At about the fifth hour a man on the second step said, out loud, to nobody, *where is he*, and the answer came back out of the floor and the answer was a place and it was wrong. **The about four hundred who came are not the about four hundred who live at the end of a made lane and are not the about four hundred that went under a second head in Harvestmonth, and the three are never added.** A man of about fifty-three with a barrow then wrote the first page out on a bare leaf and carried it to a rail of a common and read it out loud to about four people who live at the end of that lane, **and a record read out loud in a market town does not get to a made lane: a lane learns about a thing the way a lane learns about a form, from a person who came back, and what arrived at the eleventh of eleven houses was a name.** A man of about thirty-eight with a satchel asked his question of Frostmonth again and a woman answered it and the answer was wrong — that a rule a man can put his own name under is a better rule than one nobody can be shown — and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet gave the reason in three sentences and was right, and nobody agreed with him out loud. **About four of the nine people in that room said a man who publishes his own command failure is the reason the rest of the record stands up, and about five said he is the reason anybody should have believed any of it, the two sets do not overlap, and he is in neither of them and does not say which he is in.**

1286. **The panic, and the finding that a panic is a thing a room produces on its own.** In the fortnight after the reading a story went round a town of about two thousand people: that a man with a limp has published a record, that the record says where four roads are, that it has been sent to nine counties, and that the roads are shut this week. **There are no four roads in the record; the four comes from the four heads, counted by a person standing at the back of a chandler's with a loaf of bread under his arm, and a panic is a thing a room produces on its own out of a true record without anybody doing anything dishonest at all.** The man of about fifty-one in a grey coat said the room did not start it and could not find the man who did, and the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel said the people in the street were right about the one thing that matters, which is that a road anybody can find out about can be shut, and that this is not a panic talking. **A person came up that lane with a reason and asked the man of about fifty-eight whether the room was going to shut the roads, and he said no, and the person asked then what the room is for, and the man of about fifty-eight did not answer, and the reason is on the page: a question asked in a street in a market is not the same object as a question asked in a room, and a room that answers a street has been turned into a street.** Nobody was sent to find out, no order was made, no head was filled in on any form, and the chapter says on the page that a plan is a room, two clerks, a chairman with a chain of office that is a badge, a form with four heads on it and the fourth of them empty, and a story, and that that is the whole of what is on the table in this county and no chapter of the block says whose any of it is.

1287. **The cost, which is one line and is not an explanation.** In Chapter 539 a man of about thirty-four with a sheet asked Aren Kest one question and it was not about a form, and he said no, and that was the whole of the answer. He did not say why, the room did not ask, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet wrote two words on a bare leaf, turned it face down, did not read it back and has never told anybody in nine counties what the two words are. **The block's cost on the page, in one line: he made himself findable on a Saturday in Hearthmonth and about four hundred people have found him, and being found is the thing he bought, and he did not know it would be this.** He was asked nothing about a form in any chapter of the block, he conducted nothing, he got nothing back, he overrules nobody, he is not the reason anything works, and **no chapter of the block prints a paragraph accounting for his silence.** He is on the page in eight of the ten chapters under his own name, and the two he is not in are the lane and the panic, and in Chapter 535 he is in the street outside the door the reader is inside and about four hundred people go past him.

1288. **The block's end, the volume's year, and what did not move.** The first of Thawmonth, YR 320, a Monday and not a market day, and the year turns on that day and nobody in that room says so, and the clerk of about twenty-two writes the day at the top of a fresh page and does not write the year anywhere on it, and there is no head in nine counties for the year either. **The record is in public and it is true and it is complete in the only sense a man can judge, which is against his own knowledge. The hearing stands and is not adjourned, refused, retold or closed, and the man of about fifty-eight says it out loud on the first day of every month. The fourth head of the Crown's form is empty and has been for about four months and no office has filled it, and the word in a fourth head on the coast's own sheet is a word on a different document in a different county cut with a knife out of a flat stone, and the two of them have not been in one room since the twenty-fourth of Frostmonth and any chapter that puts them near each other says on the page that they are two.** The three words for one lane are still three and the room has not adopted one and nothing has been scheduled about choosing. A shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened, is not scheduled and nobody was sent. **A coast four days off has a woman of about fifty-one at a post with twenty-two notches cut in a piece of driftwood, the twenty-second is not cut, the water has not been off that mark in twenty-two years, the notches are years and are not tides, and nobody has been sent to that coast by anybody.** The block's last page is the year turned and the hearing standing and the fourth head empty and a clerk writing a day on a page, and it does not end on a person agreeing.

1289. **Hester Ollett, the one name this block spent, and why it was spent last and on her.** Forty-one, reads out loud at the chapel at the end of a made lane and at the market cross on Thursdays, asked on a Monday whether she would read a man's record of himself in a room, said she would read anything anybody asked her to read, and then asked what she was paid before she said yes. She is not a clerk, not a copyist, not a keeper, not on a sheet, not a villain and not a symbol; **she is a person whose trade is reading out loud, the trade is the one thing this volume could not say by an age and a job, and the cost of the block's public act is put on her hands and not on anybody's name.** She is not thanked in any chapter and nobody argues with her. **The name budget for Volume 11 is five: Block 0001 spent two, Block 0002 spent none, Block 0003 spent none, and this block spent one. The arithmetic is 2 + 0 + 0 + 1 = 3 of 5, and two slots remain for Batch 0005.** `Cray` was re-swept over the whole manuscript and stands at `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0254.md`.

1290. **The measurements of Batch 0004, recount on the ten files as they stand after the repair pass and carried forward from nothing.** **The block is 17,766 words in ten chapters, per chapter 2,062 / 1,904 / 1,836 / 1,669 / 1,849 / 1,838 / 1,654 / 1,668 / 1,439 / 1,847, on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file on its own and the ten added. The five hundred and thirty chapters before it recount file by file on the same basis to 1,546,100, which reproduces the Batch 0003 close exactly, and the manuscript is 1,563,866 in five hundred and forty chapters.** **The calendar, derived twice from the single anchor of day 930, which is the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318 and a Sunday, and never from one half to the other: the day-of-month half is 0 mismatches over ten datelines, the weekday half is 0, and the market-morning claim is 0, of which seven are market mornings. The ten chapter days are 1545, 1549, 1552, 1557, 1564, 1569, 1573, 1578, 1585 and 1596, the weekdays are Saturday, Wednesday, Saturday, Thursday, Thursday, Tuesday, Saturday, Thursday, Thursday and Monday, the months are Hearthmonth, Hearthmonth, Hearthmonth, Hearthmonth, Hearthmonth, Goatmonth, Goatmonth, Goatmonth, Goatmonth and Thawmonth, and the year is YR 319 in nine of the ten and turns on the tenth, on day 1596, the first of a month. No chapter of the block falls on a Sunday, so the Sunday rule and the floor that has not been washed are untouched.** **Ten footed money columns at 101, 114, 103, 126, 146, 150, 163, 167, 175 and 182 pence, ten distinct totals and ten distinct owners, 110 items in columns of 10 to 12, prices from 1 to 41 pence across 26 distinct values, every column verified against its own page for its item list, its spelled-out price list, its sum, its total and its mark conversion, and re-verified after the last prose edit. Two of the ten collided with spent totals on the first printing, 88 with `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0449.md` and 98 with `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0471.md`, and both were cleared by adding pence to the column and not by moving the number, which leaves them at 101 and 103. All ten were then checked against all ninety-three distinct numeric totals printed in the five hundred and thirty prior chapters and none collides, which is a stronger check than the hundred and twenty spent columns the standing rule names; the forty recurring totals in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are printed nowhere in this repository and were not re-read, and that is the weaker check and is stated rather than implied. The longest run one money column in this block makes against another money column in this block is **7 words**, being *a pot of tea at five, a* in Chapter 531 against Chapter 537, and against a money column in the five hundred and thirty prior chapters it is **16 words**, in Chapter 539 against `chapter-0530.md`. **This item first printed 17 and 20 and named the canon phrase as the 20-word run, and both figures failed to reproduce; the canon phrase is not in a money column at all but stands in the tail sentences after one, and its own longest shared run is the 20 words in Chapter 537 against `chapter-0526.md`.**** **No speech-length figure is printed anywhere in the block.** A sweep of the same shape returns thirteen matches for *N words* in the ten files and every one of them is a figure about a document, a short utterance or an idiom — two words written on a leaf, one correction at a reader, a nine-word notice at a door, one word said to one question, three words for one lane — and not one of the thirteen is a certification of the length of a speech the page prints. **Two were wrong on the first printing and both were caught by counting the speech and not by a sweep: Chapter 532 called an eleven-word sentence nine words, and Chapter 535 called an eighteen-word sentence nine words. The first was cut and the second was recast to nine words and re-counted at nine.** Duplication, raw token, headings stripped, bold markers removed, eight-word shingles, a file never compared with itself: **the ten new against all five hundred and thirty prior return a longest shared run of 24 words and it is the chapter-opening cost-column frame, shared with `chapter-0527.md` by `chapter-0536.md`, the run ending only because 0527 prints the eleventh column he has kept and 0536 prints the twelfth; the ten new against themselves return 20 words and it is the bold dateline frame, shared between Chapters 539 and 540. Exact duplicate paragraphs, whitespace-normalised, split on blank lines with bold markers retained, twelve words or more: 0 and 0, recounted after the last edit, and a zero for a block is not a zero for the book, because thirty paragraphs of twelve words or more stand whole in two or more different files across the manuscript in 61 chapter instances**, which is item 204 and is not repaired here. **All three of those figures moved in the review repair. The prior-chapter run was certified at 20 words with the cost-column frame named, and that was a count from one measurement attached to a name from another: the 20-word figure is real but it is the canon phrase in Chapter 537 against `chapter-0526.md`, which stands in the tail after a column and not in its item list, and the cost-column frame against Chapter 527 is 24. The self-comparison was named Chapters 532 and 538 and those share 16. The 30 reproduces on a blank-line split with bold markers retained and returns 33 with the markers stripped, 31 at a thirteen-word threshold with the markers stripped, and 43 on an every-newline split, and the split is now named because the figure was not checkable without it.** Punctuation as a count of marks actually used: **10 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 2 semicolons, 2 em dashes, 0 en dashes and 0 horizontal rules.** Per-line bold parity is even on every line of every file, the curly quotation marks balance at 111 and 111 across the block and at 25, 18, 16, 13, 4, 9, 11, 11, 2 and 2 per file, the straight-quote count is 0, and doubled words, space-before-punctuation, double blank lines and lines without a terminal mark all return 0. **`about` is 406 in 17,766, which is 22.85 per 1,000, on a case-insensitive whole-word numerator over a whitespace denominator, and 401 on a lower-case-only numerator, which is 22.57, and both are reported because the case convention is the whole difference between them; `the whole of` is 15, and a sweep for *about* standing in front of a figure of a thing somebody in the scene could have counted returns 0, and the survivors are the age house form and the honest-uncertainty register.** The barred tokens, the four barred numerals and the kin-term are at **0** across the ten files, and `stage`, `ledger`, `qualification`, `system`, `panel`, `protagonist`, `council` and `hundredweight` are at **0 at volume scope** over all forty chapters of Volume 11. A self-reference sweep across twenty-six patterns returns **0** at block scope; the nine bare-noun self-references are at **194 at manuscript scope** on a whole-word case-insensitive basis over the five hundred and forty chapter files — `this book` 127, `the volume` 23, `this volume` 13, `the block` 21, `this block` 4, `this chapter` 3, `the chapter` 2, `this batch` 1, `the batch` 0 — **and the figure inherited from the Block 0003 close was 202, the difference is eight, and it is in `the block` and `the batch`; the inherited figure was not re-derived and the eight are recorded here and not explained away.** The four-word reporting-frame family is a separate and larger count at **1,608, all of it in Volumes 04 to 08 and 10**, and the two are different objects and a number without its basis and its family beside it is a claim about a different book. **`Aren` is at 22 and `Kest` is at 22 across the ten files, in eight of the ten chapters, reported separately.** The name budget stands at **3 of 5** and this block spent one. **The lesson, and it is the same one this project has now paid for in four consecutive blocks: a claim about what a person said, did, was asked or believed is not a measurement, and every one of this block's fourteen findings was such a claim or a figure checked against something other than the prose it described, and none of the fourteen was visible to a sweep.**

1291. **The repair pass this phase ran over Batch 0004, recorded as canon because six of its findings were claims about what a person said, was asked or had done, and a claim about a person is not a measurement and no instrument in this repository can read it.** Chapter 534's dateline and its body both called one question *the only question anybody asked that day* on a page that prints three; the dateline now says one of the people in the room was not thanked for asking a question that was about a date and not about a road, and the copyist's is now *the one question that day that was about the sign*. **Chapter 537 said the clerk of about thirty-four had been asked nothing by anybody, on a page that prints him asking the woman at the rail; it now says he had nothing to give her.** Chapter 538 counted the two-sets occasions in that room as *the fourth time since the tenth of Hearthmonth*, a count no instrument owns and no page settles, and now says it is not the first. **Chapter 540 said about nine people had not been on a page in that room since the twenty-fifth of Harvestmonth, and Chapter 520 prints nine people on the wrong side of a shut road in a bold paragraph; it now says not one of them has ever come into that room.** Chapter 535 named five counts of persons and then called them four. **Chapters 535, 536 and 539 each called *the about four hundred who came to the chandler's* and *the about four hundred who live at the end of a made lane* the same figure and are not the same figure; that is the wrong pair, and the pair that is the same figure and is not the same figure is the lane's four hundred with the four hundred that went under a second head in Harvestmonth, as Chapters 508, 516 and 526 all print it.** **Chapter 536 called the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel's column the thirteenth, and the last one he keeps before it is the eleventh in Chapter 527 and none is printed between them, so it is the twelfth; Chapter 538 called the man of about fifty-one's column the first he has ever kept, and Chapters 503 and 513 print a first and a second, so it is the third.** Chapter 538 said the story came out of the chandler's *on the Thursday of last week* and the page's own dates make it a fortnight. **Chapter 534's third page had its opening pair of money items in the same order as Chapter 528's, Chapter 537's dateline ran thirty-five words into Chapter 526's, the money columns of Chapters 534 and 539 ran forty-four words together, and Chapters 536 and 540 ran twenty-five words together; all four were re-ordered or re-worded, and the block's true longest runs against the prior manuscript and inside the block are now 20 and 20 and both of them are declared frames.**

1292. **Two inherited defects this phase found and did not repair, because both of them sit in chapters that belong to an earlier batch, and Batch 0005 must not repeat either.** **First, the column ordinals of the man of about thirty-four with a sheet.** Chapter 516 prints his column as the ninth he has kept in eleven years and Chapter 524 prints it as the eleventh, and no column of his is printed between them, so one is wrong and this block could not tell which. Chapter 532 prints the thirteenth and is consistent with 524. **Second, `outline/volume-11.md` §17 prints day 1626 as the first of Mudmonth YR 320 on a Thursday, and the chain gives it a Wednesday**: day 930 is the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318 and a Sunday, every month is carried at thirty days, day 1596 is a Monday, and 1596 + 30 days is a Wednesday. The same chain gives day 1650 a Saturday, which is what the volume's own opening paragraph and the row beneath it both say, and a Thursday at 1626 would give a Sunday at 1650. **A Batch 0005 writer derives both halves from day 930 and does not copy the 1626 row.**

1293. **The second pass over this block, run by this phase after the first repair, in eight findings, and every one of them is the same class again.** Chapter 540 said *about four people were in the room at the first hour* and named four, while the same page has the clerk of about twenty-two writing the day at the top of his book at the first hour and giving that chapter its column; the room holds five and the man of about fifty-eight's speech, which is built on the difference between nine and the number present, now says five. Chapter 540 also attributed to the man of about thirty-four with a sheet a written refusal *in that lane and to nobody* on a page from Chapter 537 that prints him writing one bare leaf and reading none of it back, and the chapter now prints the leaf. **Chapter 535 gave the fourth page of the record to the man of about thirty-four with a sheet and to Hester Ollett in the same chapter, and on a page that otherwise gives the reading to her; it now gives the last of it to her alone.** Chapter 535's list of five things enumerated four and the fifth sentence was the refusal; a fifth has been put in, the number of people behind the second buttress, and Chapter 540's *five things* now agrees with it. Chapter 535 said the reader's pay *is in her column* on a column of eleven items that contains no payment. Chapter 535's money column still had an ambiguous demonstrative after the first repair, because three four-hundreds sit next to each other, and it now names the pair. Chapter 535 also said *nine hundred on a sand four days off* where every other mention in the volume says coast, and the sand is a different object with a different figure on it. **Chapter 537 supplied a mechanism that could not produce its own stock: a saddler taking the top sheet off a bundle about nine times a month, which is about thirty-six sheets in four months against about four hundred copies in four hundred kitchens; the mechanism is now every bundle that has come through that shop since Harvestmonth, and no rate is printed.** Chapter 537 also walked the clerk from the eleventh of eleven houses on down to the fourth house and then on down to the chapel, which is at the far end, and the page now says he went back up and then on down again. Chapter 539 merged the copyist's face-down leaf and the story leaf into one, told on the page what Chapter 536 said nobody would say, and then dated the copy of a first page to the Monday it had already gone out on; all three are gone. Chapter 539 also called one afternoon's cost *one line* in one bold paragraph and *a column* in another. **Chapter 536 said a woman had not answered anything in that room in four months on a page that shows questions answered four times in four chapters, and now says the thing had not happened to that question before.** Chapter 536 also said of two speakers that both were right and the woman was wrong. Chapter 533 sent the band of a hundred and four *in*, where Chapters 501, 502 and 530 all send it out, and it now goes out, and the same sentence no longer runs twenty-nine words into Chapter 501. Chapter 533 also called one printed line both cut and cast. Chapter 531 gave two counts of ten days that nothing on any page supports. Chapter 534's dateline dropped the market day its own first and last lines print. Chapter 532's dateline named three hours and the body staged a fourth, and Chapter 538's named two and the body staged a third. **And the one finding this phase did not repair: the block dates the command on the nineteenth of Mudmonth *six years ago and a little*, and `state/continuity.md` item 7 and `outline/volume-01.md` both put the Sundering in YR 301 while this volume is YR 319, and the same man is a captain by brevet at twenty-one in Chapter 532 and a man of thirty-two with a limp in the datelines, which is a difference of eleven and not of six. That figure is inherited from Chapters 504, 510, 511, 512 and 528 and it is in the Block 0004 prompt itself, so changing it in ten chapters would leave the volume worse rather than better, and it is recorded here and referred rather than silently corrected.**

1294. **The review repair of Batch 0004, run against the reviewer's six findings, and four of the six were real and two were not, and the difference between the two kinds is the whole of what this item is for.** **The reviewer reported that the longest shared run against the prior manuscript was 24 words where this project's files said 20, that the 24-word run was misidentified, that the column-against-column figure was 17 where the files say a lower number, that the self-comparison named the wrong pair of chapters, that Chapter 540's summary was stale and self-contradictory, and that the manuscript duplicate-paragraph figure was 30 where the reviewer measured 31.** **Findings four and six are confirmed outright: Chapter 540's summary did say *about four people are in the room at the first hour* and then said the clerk of about twenty-two *is one of the five*, on a page that prints five people and enumerates them, and it is repaired to five with the nine-minus-five arithmetic named; and the self-comparison named Chapters 532 and 538, which share 16 words, when the 20-word self-run is Chapter 539 against Chapter 540.** **Finding six is not a defect and the reason is a basis, not a defence: 30 is the count on a blank-line paragraph split with bold markers retained at a twelve-word threshold, it reproduces on that basis across all five hundred and forty files, and the reviewer's 31 is what the same measure returns at a thirteen-word threshold with the markers stripped, which is the fourth number in a family of four — 30, 33, 31 and 43 — all of which were run here and none of which contradicts another. The figure was not wrong; it was unnameable, because the sentence that carried it did not carry its own basis, and the repair names the split, the threshold and the marker handling in the sentence itself.** **Findings one, two and three were all one finding and the finding was real and larger than the reviewer knew.** **The block had carried 44 curly apostrophes into ten chapters, and the thirty chapters of this volume before them carry none, and every other volume of the manuscript except three is straight.** That drift is invisible to a reader and decisive to a measurement: on raw tokens the cost-column frame in Chapter 536 stands 20 words against the same sentence in `chapter-0527.md` and no further, because `satchel's` in 0536 is a curly apostrophe and `satchel's` in 0527 is a straight one and the two words are not the same word to a string comparison; fold them and the run is 24. **So the 20 this project printed was measured on the files as they stood and was true on its declared basis, and the cost-column frame it was attached to was named from a run that basis cannot see. The state file was holding a count from one measurement and a name from a different one, and that is the first time in this project that the defect has been that shape rather than an arithmetic error, and it is why a reviewer recounting the same thing honestly arrived at a different number and was right to.** **The apostrophes were folded to straight in all ten files, the two bases now agree at 24 and 24, and the prior-manuscript figure is 24 and the self-comparison is 20 between Chapters 539 and 540.** **The column figures fell with it: the longest run one money column in the block makes against another is 7 words, being *a pot of tea at five, a* in 0531 against 0537, and against a money column in the five hundred and thirty prior chapters it is 16 words, in 0539 against `chapter-0530.md`; the 17 and the 20 this project printed for those two did not reproduce on any basis, and the canon phrase it named as the 20-word run is not in a money column at all but stands in the tail sentences that follow one, its own longest shared run being the 20 words in Chapter 537 against `chapter-0526.md`.** **What was not changed: no chapter was restructured and no sentence was rewritten, and the repair to the prose is forty-four characters of punctuation across ten files, verified line by line as 37 changed lines every one of which differs from the line it replaced in nothing but apostrophe style; the block is still 17,766 words with the same per-chapter list, the quotation marks still balance at 111 and 111, the straight double-quote count is still 0, and `about` still stands at 406 and 401, `the whole of` at 15, the marks at 10 / 0 / 2 / 2 / 0 / 0, and `Aren` and `Kest` at 22 and 22 in eight chapters. The plot, the ending, the standing prohibitions and the name budget are untouched, and no controller file was opened.** **The lesson added to the four this project has already paid for twice in this block: a figure reported with a basis that does not identify the text it measures is a figure nobody can check, including the person who wrote it; and two measurements of one thing that disagree are not automatically one of them being wrong, and the third possibility, that both are right and the file has attached one to the other's name, is the one that had not been considered before and is the reason this item exists rather than a correction of a number.**

## Volume 11, Batch 0005

**The highest number in this file before this section was 1294, and this section continues from it.** Block 0005 is the last block of Volume 11 and this section is the canon it leaves. Chapters 541 to 550 are on disk, they run day 1597 to day 1650, and the manuscript was 1,588,994 words in five hundred and fifty chapters as this section was written and is 1,588,924 after the review repair recorded below, and nothing in the canon in this section moved.

1295. **The finding, stated out loud, and the four reasons the room cannot do it.** On the second of Thawmonth YR 320, a Tuesday and not a market day, the man of about fifty-eight with a chain of office that is a badge read the whole of what that room can do off a bare leaf and then put the leaf face down, and it took about four minutes and about nine people were in the room. **The finding is one sentence: an order may not act on a road without that road's own answer to a question put to it in its own words. And its second half is in the same breath and not on a second leaf: a rule about procedure is not a promise about what anybody will do, and the reason it will be read as a promise in about four years is that it is the shape of a promise, and a shape is the only part of a rule that travels.** It is on a bare leaf with no head over it and it is not in the room's book, because the room's book has one head cut into the wood over one line and that head was cut for a different thing. **Saying it is not the same as being able to do it, and the four failures are on the page in the order a man walks a road in: there is no head in nine counties for a question; there is no head in nine counties for an order and the word order is on no paper in that room; a road cannot be asked a question in its own words, because what a person will tell you is what somebody told him and the somebody is in that room or in a house in that town; and not one of nine road offices has a procedure for it.** Nothing was opened, nobody was sent, and no head on any form was filled. **The room does not vote and a vote would be a finding, and there has not been a finding in that room in five months.**

1296. **The distributed thing, and the answer to the offer, and the half leaf that came back.** On the eighth of Thawmonth, a Monday, the woman of about twenty-nine and the man of thirty-two with a limp were in one room over a weigh-house for about seven hours together for the first time since a Tuesday in Rainmonth in the year before this one, and a woman of about thirty-one with a pen was in it for all of it, **and neither of the two said one word about the four and a half years, and the narration says so once and does not say it again.** Nine leaves went out with four heads each, three filled, the fourth ruled and empty, and a question written under the fourth head by a person and not by an office, in the same words on all nine: *What is this road called, and who lives at the end of it, and what is carried on it, and what is the name you use for it?* **The challenge to the office is not a challenge to any person and is not a refusal: a question under a fourth head on a leaf is the only one of about nine documents in that county that can go out and come back with something in it that nobody in an office chose, and forwarding a question is what an office is for.** Nobody has to be sent. He cut the ninth leaf in half with a knife and gave one half to the keeper of that room and kept the other, so that neither of them has all of them, and she said that is not a reason and he said it is a precaution. **The four names that the man of about fifty-one in a grey coat offered to put on a sheet going to nine counties so that a road may complain to a person were answered on the eleventh of Thawmonth: the names go, in a sheet of oiled paper with nothing on the outside of it, and the four names are still off the letter and the letter is in a coat in that room.** The man of about fifty-one in a grey coat said out loud that the answer is worse than the letter it came in and that he is not going to argue with it and would rather be argued with than not. **And in Chapter 550 the half leaf the man of thirty-two kept came back: the man of about thirty-four with a sheet carried it down a made road himself in the last week of Thawmonth, was not sent and was not nobody-sent, it went to the landward end of a coast four days off, it came back up nineteen days or about nine more on the sixth of Mudmonth in a bundle with nine returns, and he put it on the table in a hired room over a saddler's and put it face down. It has been face down for nineteen days and nobody in that room has turned it over — not the man with the badge, not the copyist who has a knife, not the clerk whose job it is to enter things, and not the man with a barrow, who is the only person in that room who has ever been on the wrong side of a line.** Nobody has asked anybody else to and nobody has been asked. It is half a leaf and not a form, not the form, not a return and not on a bundle in nine counties; the question is under the fourth head and is not in it; the fourth head is ruled and empty; and no office has filled it and no person has and nobody in nine counties is going to.

1297. **The third asking and the answer, and the reason the answer is allowed now and was not allowed twice before.** The question *is a rule still a rule when one man changed it in a hand?* was put by the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel in Frostmonth and got nothing, and again in Goatmonth and was answered wrongly by a woman, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet gave the reason in three sentences and was right. On the eighteenth of Thawmonth, a Thursday and a market day, the man of about thirty-four with a barrow who had not been in that room before the second of a month carried at thirty, is not paid, is not a clerk and keeps no book of forms, **answered it at about the fifth hour having not been asked it: a rule is a rule when one man changed it in a hand, and a rule nobody can name the maker of is a rule anybody can be shown, and he has wanted to be shown a thing for about nine years and nobody has ever shown him one, and the reason he has now got one is not that the man was brave, it is that the man wrote his name on a page.** **The answer is allowed now because of what it is and not because of who said it, and the reason is on the page in the chairman's own mouth: a room of about nine people in a hired room over a saddler's is not a place where a thing about a rule in nine counties can be found, so the room writes the answer down because a man with a barrow said it and would not have written it down because a man with a badge said it.** He asked for three things and got all three: write it down, put no head over it, do not read it back, and he would like it in about four years to turn up in a room that is not that one with a man in it who has never heard of him. Nobody thanked him for the answer. The man of about thirty-eight with a satchel said it was the first answer the room has had in five months from somebody who is not paid, and that the smallness was in the room and not in the county, and asked for that to be written down and not said again. **The hearing stands and is not adjourned, refused, retold or closed, and there is no bench in that room and there has never been one, and nobody in that county is sentenced to anything by anything said in it, and the man of about fifty-eight says it out loud on the first day of every month and nobody has stopped him.**

1298. **The edge, and what it cost, and the woman at the eleventh house.** On the twentieth of Thawmonth, a Saturday and a market day, the man of thirty-two with a limp walked a made lane about a mile and a half out of the market town, past a common and down to eleven houses and a chapel, with nothing in his hand, and stood where the made surface stops, which is not a gate and not a stile and not a boundary stone and has never been marked, and **he did not go on, and nothing happened, and no crossing was made and none was booked, and nobody in nine counties knows he stood there except the woman of about twenty-seven, who came out with a bucket, saw him, and went back in.** **What the edge cost him is the page, and the reason is on the page: a page is a thing with one man's name at the top of it, and a man who is in two rooms at once has a page that is a thing about two men, and a thing about two men is a thing nobody can be shown. He kept the page and paid for it in about four seconds.** Beyond the end of the made lane there is a cart road about two hundred paces on that is on no sheet in nine counties and has a name about four people use and about five do not, and it is not shut and is not scheduled to be shut, and he could have gone on and did not. **The woman of about twenty-seven at the eleventh of eleven houses opened her door about two paces wide and said one thing to the lane and not to him: the cart road behind the chapel has been under water twice since the snow went and once before that, it is not on any paper anywhere, it is not a made road, nobody has been to see whether a cart goes along it, and she is not asking him to go and see, because a person who goes and sees is a person somebody sent.** He did not answer her, was not asked to, and did not go and see. She shut her door, was not thanked and did not ask to be thanked. **No crossing was conducted in this volume and none is scheduled, no mechanism is explained, no System panel is printed, and a sweep of the fifty chapters returns zero for *stage*, zero for *ledger* and zero for *qualification*.**

1299. **The finding published, and the fourth line that is not a fourth head.** On the ninth of Mudmonth, a Thursday and a market day, the copyist of about twenty-six ruled four lines on a sheet of foolscap with a straightedge and a knife, and nobody ordered it and nobody asked her to, and she said one sentence before she did it: four lines is not four heads, a head is a cut in a die and a line is a thing a person makes. **The finding went up a made road that morning in the fourth line of that sheet. The fourth line is a fourth line and not a fourth head, it is on a different paper from the Crown's form and in a different hand from the Crown's form, it is not a fifth line, and the page says so before anybody in that room can say it, and a head is a shape and not an order.** The fourth line is the only one of the four with a man's name in it, there is no head over it and nothing is ruled above or below it. Nobody in that room said the fourth head ought not to be there and nobody said it ought to be filled in, and **the fourth head of the Crown's form on the table by that door is ruled and empty and was empty at the second hour and is empty at the ninth hour and will be empty on the Monday the bundle goes, and no office in nine counties has filled it, no person has, and no person in that room filled any head on any form that day.** The bundle went up at about the ninth hour with nine returns in it and the sheet on the top of them, and a man in a saddler's shop in that town took the top sheet off it about four minutes before the copyist could get to it and put it on his board and handed the rest down, and nobody in nine counties has told that man what is on it and nobody was going to.

1300. **A man who is a public target, and what it cost him.** **The sheet of foolscap with four ruled lines on it went into that market on the ninth of Mudmonth and was read standing up in a street, and by the Tuesday about four hundred people in a town of about two thousand had read it, and on the sixteenth, the eighth day, a woman of about forty-four who sells fish came up the lane at about the fifth hour and asked the man of about fifty-eight one question, and he answered it, and about nine people heard the answer.** **About four hundred people in that market can now say where a man of thirty-two with a limp sleeps, and that is a figure of persons and it is not a figure of anybody in that room.** On the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth he came up the lane at about the third hour and there was a market in the street and he was in it and he was not upstairs; a man of about twenty-nine with a handcart knew him from a street and said the name out loud twice; and he stood in a market and let a market town say his name and did not say it back. **Nobody in that room said anything about where he was and nobody sent for him.** The man of about fifty-eight refused, in front of about nine people, to say what happens to him, and the reason is on the page: a room that says what happens to a man is a room that has started keeping a book of people, and he has a rule of his own that he does not write anybody's name on a bare leaf, and a name went into a fourth line this month on a sheet of foolscap that he did not rule and he is not going to take it out. **Nobody thanked the man with the fish, nobody thanked him, and she is not a villain, not a symbol and not a witness; she asked one question, she got an answer, and she went back to the market.**

1301. **The room's last day, and what the volume leaves on the page.** On the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth, YR 320, a Saturday and a market day, the finding went into the room's book on the thirteenth of Mudmonth with a pen and not a knife, under a head cut into the wood for a different thing, and the man of about fifty-eight said out loud that this is the correct condition for it, because a clerk in about four years is going to find a sentence under a head that was cut for something else and is going to have to decide on his own what it is about. **He also said out loud what the room cannot do, and nobody asked him to and nobody wrote it down: it cannot put a city back, it cannot open anything, it cannot send anybody to find out, and it cannot fill the fourth head, and a rule that says a thing may not be done is a different object from a thing being not done.** **The volume's resolution is that a man who has been the reason a thing went wrong can be the reason a record is worth believing, and that the two are not the same credit and are not added: about four hundred people say he is the reason the rest of it stands up and about four hundred say he is the reason anybody should have believed any of it, the two sets do not overlap, he has declined to correct either, he was asked in Block 0004 and said no, and no chapter of this block chooses between them.** The man of about fifty-one in a grey coat said one thing in about a month and it was about the room and not about a road: **the difference between the room on the second of a month carried at thirty and the room on the last day of Mudmonth is a sentence and a sheet of foolscap with four lines on it, and what has happened to it is not that it got bigger, it is that it got a man in it, and the smallness was in the room and not in the county, and he is not going to take it back and is not going to say it again.** Nobody in that room agreed with him out loud and nobody argued with him out loud. **And the inquiry did not produce a list of the four roads that are not roads, and its failure to do so is in the record as a finding and not as an excuse: nobody named them, nobody nearly named them, and about four hundred people are not on a sheet in nine counties.**

1302. **What did not move, and what the block's own hand found, in one paragraph each.** **Did not move:** the Crown's form's fourth head is empty and has been for about five months and no office has filled it; the word in a fourth head on the coast's own sheet is on a different document in a different county cut with a knife out of a flat stone; the two have not been in one room in this county since the twenty-fourth of Frostmonth; the three words for one lane are still three and the room has adopted none and nothing has been scheduled about choosing; a shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened, is not scheduled and nobody was sent, and about nine people are on the wrong side of it; about eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list; the twenty-second notch is not cut and nobody has been sent to that coast; the hearing stands; the three bars are three objects and this block touched none of them and printed no figure for any of them; the register is at seven questions and no findings, the day's book at nine entries, the count under the fourth step at nine, the bench's nine things in about nineteen years is a fourth nine, and the clerk of about twenty-two's book is a fifth, and none of the five is added to another or to a figure of persons; about nine hundred sheets a month, about a hundred and four in a band and about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month are three counts of three series and none is derived from another; the city is on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero for the whole of Volume 11 including these ten chapters; the press was not dated, the bell was not rung, no oath-stone was renewed, nothing was opened, the floor of the room over a saddler's has not been washed since the eighth of Wolfmonth and was not washed in this block; no chapter of this block falls on a Sunday; the antagonist is not named and no chapter says whose plan the inquiry is; and the name budget for the volume closed at 3 of 5 with two slots unspent. **The fourteen findings this phase made are at `state/batch-summaries.md` under this block's heading and are the same class the project has now paid for in six consecutive blocks: three wrong weekdays, one barred money total, one collision with a spent column, one column that did not foot, three wrong column ordinals, fifteen narrator self-references, one occurrence of the retired four-word reporting frame, 67 curly apostrophes in a volume whose first forty chapters carry none, a 132-word paragraph duplicated whole between two chapters, a chapter with no quoted speech in it, two doubled words, and a duplicate figure produced by a broken instrument that compared a one-word tuple against a set of eight-word tuples.** *The standing lesson is unchanged and it is the only one that matters here: a claim about what a person said, did, was asked or believed is not a measurement, and a figure computed by an instrument that has not been shown to advance its own window is not a measurement either.*

## The Volume 11 Batch 0005 review repair

**The highest number in this file before this section was 1302, and this section continues from it. Nothing in the canon above this line changed. The plot, the ending, the dates, the weekdays, the money, the names, the prohibitions and the antagonist are exactly as items 1295 to 1302 left them, and the review of the block confirmed all of that before it found anything else. The review record itself is `reviews/volume-11/batch-0005.md`.**

1303. **The review's six findings, and the disposition of each, and the two the repair found after the review was given.** **The review ran against the files and changed nothing, and every mechanical figure the block's own records printed reproduced exactly on re-measurement: the block word count, the manuscript total, the ten money columns footing item for item and total for total and conversion for conversion, the ten datelines deriving on both halves from the day-930 anchor, the four duplicate-paragraph bases, the per-file quotation balances, the apostrophe convention and the barred tokens. The findings were all of the class this project has paid for in six consecutive blocks, and not one of them was a plot defect, a date, a weekday, a money total, a canon figure or a name.** **Finding one, and the most serious of the six, is that the phrase *a figure of a person standing four lines from another figure of a person is the defect no instrument in this project owns* stands verbatim in the bolded narration of `chapter-0547.md` and again in `chapter-0548.md`, lifted word for word out of the block's own prompt, and that *this project* is authorial language inside a register that has no such words in it. Both are revoiced in the room's terms and both keep every fact they carried: that the two of them were in one room saying it aloud, that the two of them are a woman of about twenty-nine and a woman of about thirty-one, that a leaf which sets one of them down cannot hold the other one four lines away from her, and that everybody in that room could see it and nobody could stop it.** **Finding two is that `chapter-0548.md` carried a whole bold paragraph whose content is the non-existence of a man of about thirty-three, which then explained its own arrangement, and that this is the outline-prose-in-a-body's-clothes failure the block's own prompt names as the fourth of four. It is gone, and the paragraph that replaces it is four people doing four different physical things because of what was decided: the clerk of about twenty-two lays both hands flat on the open book, the copyist of about twenty-six puts the knife down on the sill and does not pick it up again that morning, and the man of about fifty-three with a barrow puts his coat on over his shirt and says nothing about where he is going and is not asked.** **A third instance of the same construction in the same chapter, a cost column attributed to a man of about fifty-four with a satchel who is not in the room, is trimmed to the column's actual owner, the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel, and the attribution keeps its function.** **Finding three is that the certification *the retired four-word reporting frame is at 0* was a string match on four enumerated forms and was not a family sweep, and that the frame stood at six across five chapters in a fifth form, *have it since that*, at Chapters 544, 545 twice, 546, 547 and 549. All six are revoiced; the family is now swept as `\w+ since (that|then)` and is at 0 at block scope and 0 across all fifty chapters of Volume 11.** **Finding four is that *a self-reference sweep across twenty-six patterns is at 0 at block scope* was false, because `this project` was not among the twenty-six and occurred twice. The pattern list is now named in `state/current.md` and `this project` is in it and the sweep returns 0.** **Finding five is that the prompt's hard cap of one use per chapter on *about four of the nine thought X and about five thought Y* was broken twenty times across Chapters 543, 544, 548 and 549 and that the certification was silent about the cap, and the review's own count of 20 reproduces exactly on a recount. The device now stands at one use in each of Chapters 541, 542, 543, 544, 546, 547, 548, 549 and 550 and at none in 545, and every chapter over the cap was revoiced rather than cut: the room is still divided and the two sets still do not overlap, but where the division was carried by the figure of nine standing in front of it two or three times, it is carried by *half* and *the other half*, and in Chapter 543 and Chapter 544 the sentence that held the device now holds a different clause with the same sense.** **Every one of the four chapters keeps a consequence on the page for the use it retains, and the uses that were cut took their consequences with them: the man with the barrow still gets up and puts his coat on, the man of about thirty-one with a pen still writes a bare leaf and rules nothing, and the fish in Chapter 549 still goes back down the lane and the story still goes round the market faster than she carried it.** **Finding six, that the prompt is the upstream cause of finding one, is disposed of in the successor prompt rather than in the batch prompt, which is a record of what was handed to a writer and is not edited after the fact; `workspace/volume-11/close/PROMPT.md` now carries a standing rule that no chapter card may hand the writer a phrase in the author's register to print.** **The two the repair found are both figures about a person, which is the class the block's own prompt says no instrument in this project owns. In `chapter-0549.md` a woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key said that *about four days ago a man of thirty-four with a satchel asked him one question and he said one word, and the word was no*: the one-word answer is Chapter 539's and the asker there is the man of about thirty-four with a **sheet**, and Chapter 539 is dated Goatmonth the twentieth, YR 319, which is day 1526, and Chapter 549 is day 1641, so the interval is a hundred and fifteen days and is about four months and not about four days. The satchel is also wrong for that man: the man with a satchel is thirty-eight in forty-nine places in this volume and thirty-four in five, all five of them in Chapters 517 to 519 and inherited from Block 0002, so the sheet is the correct object and the thirty-four is correct with it. In `chapter-0543.md` a man who is narrated as having said three things is described four lines later by a figure of a person as *a man who says nine things and is right about all of them*, and it is three.**

1304. **Every figure the repair moved, recounted on the files after the last prose edit, and the figures it did not move recounted again.** **The block is 25,058 words, per chapter 2,537 / 2,714 / 2,314 / 2,405 / 2,344 / 2,387 / 2,293 / 2,376 / 2,417 / 3,271, on the declared basis of a plain whitespace split of each file with the title line included and the ten added; the five hundred and forty chapters before it recount to 1,563,866 and the manuscript is 1,588,924 in five hundred and fifty chapters. The block was 25,128 and the manuscript 1,588,994 and the whole of the difference is the seventy words the eight repairs removed.** **Money: ten footed columns at 113, 121, 131, 141, 155, 171, 208, 186, 194 and 226 pence, every one re-footed item for item, sum for sum, total for total and mark-conversion for mark-conversion against its own page after the repair, and none of the ten moved, because no repair touched an item list.** **The longest run one money column in the block makes against another in the block is 7 words and reproduces; against a money column in the five hundred and forty prior chapters the certification printed 16 and the true figure is 8, being *a third pot of tea at three, a* in Chapter 542 against `chapter-0516.md`, and 16 is Block 0004's own figure carried forward without being counted.** **Punctuation: 8 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 2 semicolons, 3 em dashes, 0 en dashes, 0 rules; the em dashes were 5 and the two that went stood in the paragraph that named a man who was not in the room. Curly quotation marks balance at 100 and 100 and at 10 / 21 / 6 / 14 / 1 / 10 / 9 / 5 / 15 / 9 per file, straight quotes 0, curly apostrophes 0, straight apostrophes 102, bold parity even on every line of every file, doubled words 0, space-before-punctuation 0, double blank lines 0.** **`about` is 672 in 25,058, which is 26.82 per 1,000 on a case-insensitive whole-word numerator over a whitespace denominator, and 670, which is 26.74, on a lower-case-only one; it was 701 in 25,128 at 27.86 and 699 at 27.78, and the 29 tokens that moved are almost entirely the device that was cut back to one use per chapter. `the whole of` is 30 and did not move.** **The calendar did not move: the ten datelines still derive on both halves from day 930 with 0 / 0 / 0 mismatches, the chapter days are still 1597, 1603, 1606, 1613, 1615, 1622, 1627, 1634, 1641 and 1650, the year is YR 320 in all ten, eight are market mornings and none falls on a Sunday.** **The barred tokens, the four barred numerals and the kin-term are at 0 across the ten files, and `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` `protagonist` `council` `hundredweight` are at 0 across all fifty chapters. `Aren` 12 and `Kest` 12. Quoted spans 10 / 21 / 6 / 14 / 1 / 10 / 9 / 5 / 15 / 9, and no repair changed the number of spans in any chapter.**

1305. **The duplication figures, which is where this block's own certification was weakest and where the repair found a defect the review had not reported.** **The certification said *raw token with punctuation retained, headings stripped, bold markers removed, eight words or more, a file never compared with itself* and printed 26 words for the ten new against the five hundred and forty prior. Those are two different measurements wearing one name. On the basis as declared — headings stripped, the double-asterisk markers removed and the single-asterisk emphasis markers kept — the figure is 25. On the basis that removes both marker kinds it is 26, and 26 is the one the instrument that produced the number was actually running. The name was attached to the other number, which is the exact defect `state/continuity.md` 1294 records for Batch 0004 in the same files and the same register, and it is the second time this volume has shipped a duplication figure whose stated basis does not produce it.** **The basis is corrected to *emphasis markers removed* and the figure is 26, and the narrow basis and its 25 are printed beside it so that a successor can rebuild either. The run is the same on both: the four lines from the coast in Chapter 547 against `chapter-0525.md`, which are the same four lines and are meant to be, and which is the one run above twenty words in the block that is a document and not a frame.** **The self-comparison is 23 words and the certification named one run for it, the money-column tail, and there are five pairs that reach 23: the money-column tail in 0544 against 0545, the chapter-opening frame in 0541 against 0546 and against 0550, the chapter-opening frame in 0545 against 0549, and the bare-leaf frame in 0546 against 0549. The figure is right and the name was one of five, and it is now five.** **The exact-duplicate-paragraph figures all four reproduce after the repair, unchanged, which is the check that says the repair did not touch them: 31 paragraphs in 63 chapter instances on a blank-line split with bold markers retained at twelve words, 32 in 65 with the markers stripped, 30 in 61 at thirteen words with the markers stripped, and 44 in 95 on an every-newline split; 0 duplicates among the ten new and 1 between the ten new and the manuscript, being the question printed identically in `chapter-0536.md` and `chapter-0544.md` because it is the same question asked three times. The manuscript-scope 31 in 63 against the inherited 30 in 61 is one paragraph and two instances, being the single new duplicate this block created, and the difference is accounted for and not explained away.**

1306. **What this repair adds to the pattern, and it is a new class and the plainest one in the file.** **A prompt can hand a writer a phrase in the author's register and the writer will print it, and no sweep for out-of-world vocabulary will catch it, because the words are ordinary English and every one of them is a word this book uses.** *This project* is not a System word, not a mechanic, not a stage direction and not on any barred list in this repository, and the block's barred-token sweep returned clean on two chapters that were printing it. The sweep that would have caught it is not a vocabulary list at all; it is a rule about register, and a rule about register cannot be enforced by a token count, which is the whole reason it took a reader and not an instrument. **And the second thing is the older lesson arriving one item later: three of the eight findings are a number in a state file that was inherited from an earlier block instead of counted, and a fourth is a number in a state file that is right and is attached to a name from a different measurement. Both are the failure this project has now paid for in seven consecutive blocks, and both live in the file a phase reads first and trusts, which is why a close that audits a volume by comparing its state files against each other finds nothing.** The standing rule after this pass is the one Volume 07's Batch 0004 review wrote down and this file has repeated since: **print the basis, print both numbers when two measurements disagree, and never let a phase inherit a figure from a state file it has not counted on the files.** And one addition to it: **a pattern list that is not printed cannot be checked, and a sweep of twenty-six unnamed patterns that returns 0 is a claim about a list nobody can see, which is why the list is now written into `state/current.md` in full.**


## Volume 11 Close — items 1307 to 1316, and the highest number in this file is now 1316

**The highest number in this file before this section was 1306, and this section continues from it. Nothing in the canon above this line changed. The plot, the ending, the dates, the weekdays, the money, the names, the prohibitions, the name budget and the antagonist are exactly as items 1295 to 1306 left them. The close record is `outline/volumes/volume-11-close.md` and it is the eleventh document of its kind; `reviews/volume-11/batch-0005.md` remains the durable record of the block review and is not superseded, only extended.**

1307. **The close ran five checks over all fifty chapters and the calendar, the money, the speech figures, the duplication and the hygiene all return clean, and the clean results are the finding.** The chain was rebuilt from the single anchor of **day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday**, on the carried thirty-day month, and the weekday half and the day-of-month half were derived separately from the number and never from each other. **All fifty datelines foot on month, day of month, weekday and market flag with zero mismatches on all four, and the two half-derivations were run separately and returned 0 and 0 and a market claim of 0 as three figures and not as one. The volume runs day 1381 to day 1650, which is 269 days elapsed and 270 inclusive, and the year turns on day 1596, which is the first of a month and the last day of Block 0004, and every date from that day on is YR 320.** Thirty-four of the fifty chapters fall on a market morning, at 8 / 6 / 5 / 7 / 8 by block, out of seventy-eight market days in the whole span at thirty-nine Saturdays and thirty-nine Thursdays. **All fifty money columns foot item for item, sum for sum, total for total and mark-conversion for mark-conversion, at 944 item lines and prices from 1 to 41 pence and a mark at forty-eight pence in every conversion, and there is one collision in the volume, 101 pence at `chapter-0506.md` and at `chapter-0531.md`.**
